Chapter 3 – In the Polling Room  
 
A. Who Is Allowed in the Polling Room? 
(SecƟons 102.031(3), 101.131(4), 101.58 Fla. Stat.) 
While the polls are open, the only persons allowed in the polling room on ElecƟon Day are:  
 Poll workers wearing ID badges. 
 The Duval County Supervisor of ElecƟons or personnel (i.e., staﬀ, Rovers, or technicians) wearing ID 
badges. 
 Voters who are present to vote. 
 A person (such as an elderly person or a child) in the care of a voter.  
 A person caring for a voter or assisƟng a voter (i.e., someone assisƟng a voter who cannot read or does 
not speak English or assisƟng an elderly person or a person with disabiliƟes).  
 Poll Watchers approved by the Duval County Supervisor of ElecƟons wearing ID badges. See Poll 
Watchers (p. 19). 
 ElecƟon observers appointed by the Department of State.  
 Law enforcement oﬃcers or emergency service personnel with permission of the Manager or a majority 
of the ElecƟon Board. 
 
The public and the media are allowed to enter the polling room and watch the procedures before the 
polls open and aŌer the polls close and aŌer all voters have cast their ballots. They may observe poll 
workers opening and closing the precinct. No photography is allowed. SecƟon 100.011 (2) Fla. Stat. 
Do not allow anyone to touch equipment without displaying an SOE ID badge. If in doubt, call the 
Manager Hotline. 
 
B. Who Is Not Allowed in the Polling Room?  
(SecƟons 102.031(4), 102.031(3)a Fla. Stat.) 
While the polls are open, the following persons are not allowed in the polling room on ElecƟon Day:   
 Candidates, except to vote  
 Members of the media, except to vote  
 Law enforcement oﬃcers or emergency service personnel in a capacity other than as a voter, unless 
permiƩed by the Manager or a majority of the ElecƟon Board 
 
If the polling room is in a locaƟon commonly used by the public to gain access to businesses or homes (such as the 
lobby of a condominium) or in an area tradiƟonally used as public area for discussion (such as a mall), there may 
be other people traveling through the area. Monitor the polling room to ensure that no one interferes with the 
voƟng process. 
 
Copies of SecƟon 102.031, Fla. Stat. are in the Voter InformaƟon Binder for distribuƟon to individuals or 
groups demanding legal proof that they may not enter the polling room.  
 
Do not allow, under any circumstances, media with or without cameras inside the polling room during 
voƟng hours from 7:00 a.m. unƟl aŌer polls close at 7:00 p.m. and all voters have cast their ballots.  
 

 
19 
 
C. Voters 
(Rule 1S-2.034, SecƟon 102.031(5), Fla. Stat.) 
Voters may bring in pre-marked sample ballots or campaign literature for their personal use. See Voter Marks 
Ballot in VoƟng Booth (p. 34). They may not use these to campaign inside the polling place or within 150 feet of 
the entrance to the polling place. Voters may wear campaign buƩons, shirts, hats, or any other campaign items 
when they enter the polling place to vote.  
 
 
D. Poll Watchers 
(SecƟons 101.131, 101.111, 101.23, 102.031(3), Fla. Stat.) 
Each poliƟcal party and each candidate may have one Poll Watcher in each polling room when the polls are open. 
AddiƟonally, a poliƟcal commiƩee formed for the speciﬁc purpose of expressly advocaƟng the passage or defeat 
of an issue on the ballot may also have a designated Poll Watcher in each polling room when polls are open. 
 
Approved Poll Watchers are allowed inside the polling places in their designated county. However, each poliƟcal 
party, candidate, or poliƟcal commiƩee may have only one Poll Watcher present at any one Ɵme in each polling 
room when the polls are open. Poll Watchers must be registered voters of Duval County and they may not be law 
enforcement oﬃcers or a candidate on the ballot. The Duval County Supervisor of ElecƟons will provide each 
polling place with a list of names of the preapproved Poll Watchers. AddiƟonal poll watcher informaƟon is 
located in the Voter InformaƟon Binder. 
 
Poll Watcher Guidelines 
 
Poll Watchers may: 
 Observe the conduct of the elecƟon from setup unƟl breakdown of the polling place.  
 Check in with the Manager upon arrival and show ID badge and photo idenƟﬁcaƟon for veriﬁcaƟon of 
name on the Poll Watchers List in the AdministraƟve Folder. They must wear the ID badges provided to 
them by the ElecƟons Oﬃce at all Ɵmes while in the polling place.  
 Observe the voter check-in process and inspect and record the names of voters from the precinct 
register if their acƟons do not interfere with the precinct staﬀ checking in voters.  
 Furnish their own materials and necessiƟes. 
 Submit wriƩen voter challenges to the Manager. They must inform the Manager if they wish to challenge 
a voter’s right to vote. The Manager will follow the procedures for a challenged voter.  
 Sit or stand conveniently near the precinct staﬀ as they issue ballots and aid at the Help Desk. 
 Pose quesƟons regarding polling place procedures to the Manager for resoluƟon. They must inform the 
Manager if they see anything that does not comply with elecƟon laws.   
 Bring in and use mobile electronic devices in a non-disrupƟve way.  
 
Poll Watchers may not: 
 Speak to or interact with voters or poll workers inside the polling room (other than the Manager). 
 Come closer to poll workers or the voƟng booths than reasonably necessary to perform their funcƟons. 
 Wander around the polling room or ask quesƟons of poll workers other than the Manager.   
 Wear campaign buƩons, shirts, hats, or other campaign items.  
 Help voters who need assistance in the polling place.  
 
20 
 
 Produce noise, take photographs or recordings, or have audible conversaƟons on their mobile electronic 
devices. 
 Obstruct the orderly conduct of any elecƟon. They must be respecƞul and mindful of the service being 
provided to the voters.   
 
For all potenƟal violaƟons, the Manager should call the Manager Hotline for validaƟon and provide the name and 
ID badge number of the Poll Watcher. All Poll Watchers may be given one warning by the Duval County Supervisor 
of ElecƟons Oﬃce. If there is another potenƟal violaƟon, call the Manager Hotline again. 
 
E. Order at the Polls 
(SecƟon 102.031, Fla. Stat.) 
The Duval County Supervisor of ElecƟons is governed by Florida statutes to maintain order at the polls and to 
enforce elecƟon laws. The Duval County Supervisor of ElecƟons and the ElecƟon Board may take any reasonable 
acƟon to ensure order is maintained at the polling place. Law enforcement oﬃcers may be called upon to 
remove disrupƟve persons from the polling room or the No SolicitaƟon Zone.  
 
DepuƟes are appointed by the Jacksonville Sheriﬀ to assist in maintaining order outside the polling room but 
within the No SolicitaƟon Zone. DepuƟes are sworn in aŌer compleƟon of training and are supervised by 
Managers. DepuƟes can assist the Manager with maintaining order inside the polling room when instructed by 
the Manager.  
 
It is possible that protesters show up at your polling place. Protesters must stay outside the 150-foot boundary 
marked by orange cones. Outside the 150-foot radius, the Deputy has no authority. If protesters refuse to abide 
by the elecƟon laws, alert the Manager. 
 
F. Photography Prohibited 
(SecƟon 102.031, Fla. Stat.; DE AO 12-13) 
No photography is allowed in the polling room except as indicated below. This prohibiƟon also applies to 
operaƟonal security cameras within the polling room. If they remain operaƟonal, they must be covered so 
voters, voƟng, and ballots are not recorded in violaƟon of privacy. If cameras are not operaƟonal, a noƟce is 
posted on the Sign Kiosk noƟfying voters that the camera is not in use during voƟng hours.  
 
Voters are permiƩed to photograph only their own ballot.   
 
 
G. Solicitation Restrictions 
(SecƟons 101.051(2), 102.031, Fla. Stat.) 
Exit polling by media or others is an excepƟon to the no solicitaƟon law. Exit Pollsters may approach voters only 
aŌer voters leave the polling place. DepuƟes will ensure that interviews are only conducted with voters exiƟng 
the site, not entering. Otherwise, no person or group may solicit voters within the designated No SolicitaƟon Zone 
or inside the polling place. 
“Solicit” and “solicitaƟon” includes:  
 Asking someone for their vote  
 Oﬀering to assist someone to vote  

 
21 
 
 Asking for someone’s opinion  
 Asking for a contribuƟon  
 Displaying poliƟcal adverƟsements or campaign materials, including signage on vehicles, is prohibited—
except for voters who enter and exit the polling place directly to vote without loitering 
 DistribuƟng any poliƟcal or campaign material or handout  
 ConducƟng a poll (exit polling excepted)  
 Asking someone to sign a peƟƟon  
 Selling or aƩempƟng to sell any type of item  
 Oﬀering food in exchange for an “I Voted” sƟcker 
 Giving water or food 
 Engaging in any acƟvity with the intent to inﬂuence a voter 
  
 
22 
 
Chapter 4 – Voting Process 
 
Inspector Techs may check in voters aŌer the Manager announces the polls are open at 7:00 a.m. 
 
A. Photo Identiﬁcation 
(SecƟons 101.043, 101.23, 101.49, Fla. Stat.) 
 
 
The Florida Statutes state that a voter must present a current and valid picture idenƟﬁcaƟon to vote. Valid ID 
Sheets are located in the AdministraƟve Folder. Electronic IDs and photocopies of IDs are not acceptable. A Voter 
Valid ID Sheet 
 
23 
 
InformaƟon Card is not a valid form of ID. Only the following forms of current and valid photo ID are acceptable by 
law:  
 Florida driver license  
 Florida ID card issued by the Department of Highway Safety and Motor Vehicles  
 U.S. passport or U.S. passport card 
 Debit/credit card  
 Military ID  
 Student ID  
 ReƟrement center ID  
 Neighborhood associaƟon ID  
 Public assistance ID   
 Veteran health ID card issued by the U.S. Department of Veterans Aﬀairs  
 License to carry a concealed weapon or ﬁrearm issued pursuant to secƟon 790.06, Fla. Stat.  
 Employee ID card issued by any branch, department, agency, or enƟty of the federal government, state, 
county, or a municipality 
 
If the photo ID presented does not contain the voter’s signature, a separate ID with the voter’s 
signature is required (i.e., a student ID with a photo AND a credit card or other idenƟﬁcaƟon such as a 
library card with the voter’s signature). 
 
B. Voter at Check-In Station 
(SecƟons 98.461, 101.5608(1), 101.043(1), 101.045, 119.07 Fla. Stat.) 
Inspector Techs at the Check-in StaƟon should signal the next person in line and say the following: “Welcome! 
May I please have your photo and signature ID?”  
If the person presents a valid photo ID and signature, compare the person in the photo to the voter:   
 If determined to be the same person, proceed to check in the voter. 
 If you doubt they are the same person, direct the voter to the Help Desk.  
If the person does not present a valid photo ID and signature, direct the voter to the Help Desk. 
Problems with ID? No voter should be turned away from voƟng for lack of photo idenƟﬁcaƟon. If the 
voter does not present both the proper photo and signature ID, the voter may be allowed to vote a 
provisional ballot.  
 
Inspector Techs check in voters on the EViD electronic poll book by 
following the procedures below.  
 
Using a Scanner to Search for a Voter 
A scanner should be used to search for a voter. In most cases, 
voters will provide a valid Florida driver license or Florida ID card 
which may be scanned by the EViD using the scanner plugged into 
an EViD at the Check-in StaƟon or the scanner aƩached to the right 
side of the Edge tablet at the Help Desk. The scanner should be 
plugged into an available USB port on the back of the EViD and 
must be plugged in prior to turning on the EViD. 
Scanner 
 
24 
 
To use the scanner, posiƟon the ID 4-6” from the scanner. You will hear a sound alerƟng you that the ID has been 
successfully scanned. If you experience any issues with the use of the scanner, select “More” on the Home screen, 
then select “Reset Scanner,” select “OK” and try to use the scanner again.  
Once the ID card is scanned, the voter’s registraƟon informaƟon will appear on the screen. When you have 
veriﬁed the correct voter registraƟon record, select it from the list. If a grid of voter records appears, verify the 
ﬁrst and last name and the date of birth. If the voter is not using a valid Florida driver license or Florida ID card, 
then you must do a manual search. 
Expired ID 
If the EViD alerts you that the voter’s Florida driver license or Florida ID card is expired, advise the voter that they 
must present a current and valid ID. Select “OK” and then scan another ID or use manual search if the ID cannot 
be scanned. If the voter does not have a valid ID, direct them to the Help Desk. 
 
Out of State ID 
If the EViD alerts you that the voter’s ID is not a Florida driver license or Florida ID card, check the ID. If it is not a 
Florida driver license or Florida ID card, advise the voter that they must present a current and valid ID. Select “OK” 
and then scan another ID or use manual search if the ID cannot be scanned. If the ID is a Florida driver license or 
Florida ID card, select “OK” and then perform a manual search. If the voter does not have a valid ID, direct them 
to the Help Desk. 
 

 
25 
 
Using Manual Search to Search for a Voter  
If the voter’s valid ID cannot be scanned, look up the voter on the EViD by using a manual search: 
 
 Select “Voter Check-in Search” on the Home screen. 
 Enter the voter’s date of birth in MM/DD/YYYY format. 
 Enter the voter’s iniƟals (last and ﬁrst) in the correct ﬁelds.  
 Select “Search” and the voter’s informaƟon will likely appear on the screen.  
 If you get the results grid with several voters visible, select the correct voter by verifying the ﬁrst and last 
names and the date of birth. Select the down arrow to see addiƟonal grid results. When you have veriﬁed 
the correct voter registraƟon record, select it from the list. 
 You may also search by voter registraƟon number, address, or ﬁrst name and date of birth.  
 If the voter found is not the voter you are searching for, select “Back” to try again. 
 If no voters are found, select “OK” to try again. 
 
 

 
26 
 
If the voter’s name is not found, check for name variaƟons.

Voter Check-In Using the EViD Electronic Poll Book 
When you have found the voter on the EViD, compare the voter’s address on their ID to the address on the EViD. 
Ask the voter: “Do you have an address change or update to make?” If the addresses match and the voter does 
not indicate a change, select “Yes” on the screen below to check in the voter.   
 
 
 
If the addresses do NOT match and the voter does not indicate a change, ask the voter to recite their address. If 
the address recited matches the address on the EViD, select “Yes” and check in the voter. 

 
27 
 
If the address on the ID does NOT match and the address recited does NOT match, or if the voter requests an 
address change or update, select “No.” 
 
 
When asked, “Do you want to do a Name/Address change?”, select “Yes.” You will be prompted to refer the voter 
to the clerk. Select “OK.” A Help Desk Ticket will print. Give the Ɵcket to the voter and direct the voter to the Help 
Desk.  
 
 
Voter Signature Using the EViD Electronic Poll Book 
Now the voter must sign the signature pad. Say the following to the 
voter: “Please sign on the line using the stylus or your ﬁnger.” 
Compare the voter’s signature on the EViD with the signature on the 
ID. If the signatures appear to be the same, then select “Accept.” 
During in-person voƟng, the voter is standing in front of us, and we 
can verify their idenƟty largely by comparing them to their picture. 
However, the signature becomes more important should the photo be 
in quesƟon.  

 
28 
 
A voter’s signature on a signature pad will rarely be the same as on their ID. However, they 
should be reasonably close. If the signature on the EViD is very diﬀerent from the ID, select 
“Clear” and say to the voter: “Would you please sign again and try to make your signature 
look more like the signature on your ID?” The second signature should be acceptable. 
However, if a voter has had a situaƟon that has caused their signature to change, select 
“Reject.” When prompted to conﬁrm you wish to reject, select “Yes.” A Help Desk Ticket will 
print. Give the Ɵcket to the voter and direct the voter to the Help Desk. 
AŌer accepƟng the voter’s signature, a VoƟng Pass will print from the EViD. Give the VoƟng 
Pass and the voter’s ID to the voter and say the following: “Here is your ID and VoƟng Pass. 
Please put your ID away – you will not need it again. Please take your VoƟng Pass to the 
Ballot StaƟon (point to the table) and they will give you your ballot.” 
Remember to always thank every voter pleasantly aŌer checking them in and prinƟng their 
VoƟng Pass. Say, “Thank you for coming out to vote today. If you need help with anything, 
just ask.”  
There is no way for an Inspector Tech to check in a voter or issue a VoƟng Pass to a voter who is not 
eligible or whose eligibility to vote is in quesƟon. Instead, the EViD will print a Help Desk Ticket. 
 
Voter Doesn’t Want to Sign Signature Pad 
If a voter does not want to sign the electronic signature pad, you can select 
“Accept” with nothing marked on the signature pad. When the EViD asks if 
you want to accept the blank signature, select “Yes.” The VoƟng Pass will 
print, and the voter will need to sign their VoƟng Pass on the line provided 
before taking it to the Ballot StaƟon. If the voter refuses to sign either the 
VoƟng Pass or the signature pad, they may not be issued a ballot. Direct 
this voter to the Help Desk to learn more. 
 
Voter Not Found 
If you cannot ﬁnd the voter aŌer a thorough search for the voter using all the 
methods above, select “Voter Not Found.” You will be prompted to refer the voter to 
the clerk. Select “OK.” A Help Desk Ticket will print. Return the voter’s ID, give the 
Help Desk Ticket to the voter, and direct the voter to the Help Desk.  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 Help Desk Ticket 
VoƟng Pass 

 
29 
 
Eligible for Assistance  
Some voters indicated that they were eligible for 
assistance when ﬁlling out or updaƟng their Voter 
RegistraƟon ApplicaƟon. For these voters, the electronic 
poll book will indicate “Eligible for Assistance.” Select 
“OK.” Do not menƟon this to the voter. If they say 
nothing say, “Thank you for coming out to vote today. If 
you need help with anything, just ask.” If they don’t 
request assistance, strike out the message printed on the 
VoƟng Pass before giving it to the voter and direcƟng 
them to the Ballot StaƟon. If they request assistance, 
direct them to the Help Desk. For more informaƟon, see 
Assistance at the Polls (p. 43).  
 
Voter Has Already Voted  
AŌer voter search and address veriﬁcaƟon, the EViD may 
show a message that the voter has already voted. Say, 
“The system is telling me that you have already voted in 
this elecƟon. Did you send in a vote-by-mail ballot, or 
did you vote at an Early VoƟng locaƟon?” If the voter 
remembers voƟng, then they may exit the polling room. 
Remember to give them back their ID before they leave. 
Select “No” when prompted to issue a provisional ballot. 
When asked if you want to cancel and return to Home 
screen, select “Yes.” If the voter insists that they have not 
already voted, select “Yes” when prompted to issue a 
provisional ballot. A Help Desk Ticket will print. Return 
the voter’s ID, give the Ɵcket to the voter, and direct the 
voter to the Help Desk.  
Wrong Precinct  
If a voter is in the wrong polling place, the EViD will give 
you the opƟon to print an AcƟon Required Ticket. When 
asked if the voter wishes to vote at their correct polling 
place, select “Yes” to print the Ɵcket. Give the AcƟon 
Required Ticket to the voter, along with their ID, and say, 
“You are not in your assigned polling place. This Ɵcket 
shows the name and address of your correct locaƟon. 
You may bring this Ɵcket with you for your 
convenience.” Do not engage in further conversaƟon 
about their assigned polling place. They may contact the 
Duval County Supervisor of ElecƟons at (904) 255-VOTE 
[8683] if they have further quesƟons. If the voter insists 
on voƟng at the incorrect locaƟon, direct the voter to the 
Help Desk. 
If an ineligible voter is in the wrong polling place, the EViD will display the reason why the voter is 
ineligible. Advise the voter of the ineligibility reason and then give them the AcƟon Required Ɵcket. 

 
30 
 
 
Voter Status is Inactive  
AŌer searching for and ﬁnding a voter, the EViD may show a message that 
the voter’s status is “InacƟve.” This means that some elecƟon mail has been 
returned. Do not say anything to the voter. Process the voter normally. 
 
 
 
Protected Addresses 
If a voter’s address is marked as “Address Protected,” do not ask the voter 
what they do for work or ask why their address is protected informaƟon. 
Use the regular procedure for checking in a voter on the EViD. Because you 
cannot see their address on the screen, you need to ask the voter, “Do you 
have an address change or update to make?” If the voter’s informaƟon has 
changed, direct the voter to the Help Desk.  
 
Other Voter Status 
For any other voter status message (“Challenged,” “ID Required,” “Pending,” 
“Ineligible,” etc.), you will be prompted to refer the voter to the clerk aŌer 
name and address veriﬁcaƟon. Select “Yes.” A Help Desk Ticket will print. 
Return the voter’s ID, give the Ɵcket to the voter, and direct the voter to the 
Help Desk. 
 
 
 
Voters have a right to vote a provisional ballot in certain circumstances, which is an opƟon provided 
under both federal and state law. Never deny a voter the opportunity to cast a provisional ballot. 
Provisional voters are processed at the Help Desk. 
  

 
31 
 
Undo Voter Check-In  
If a voter leaves without voƟng or if the wrong voter is mistakenly checked in (but has not yet voted), a voter’s

2. From the Other AcƟons screen, select “Undo Check-in.”  
 
 
3. From the drop-down menu select a reason for the Undo Ticket and press “OK.” 
 LeŌ Without VoƟng – a voter was checked in but leaves before casƟng a ballot and their idenƟty is 
known  
 Wrong Voter – a voter was checked in mistakenly while aƩempƟng to check in the correct voter 
 Other

4. An Undo Ticket will print automaƟcally.

C. Eligible Voter at Ballot Station     
(SecƟons 101.045, 101.5608, Fla. Stat.)  
If a voter has been checked in and has received a VoƟng Pass, issue the voter a ballot at the Ballot StaƟon. It is 
important to verify and give voters the correct ballot style because it allows the voter to cast a ballot with the 
candidates that represent the area in which they live. Never leave the Ballot StaƟon unaƩended. One ballot pad 
of each ballot style may be on the Ballot StaƟon table at a Ɵme. 
 
1. Say the following to the voter: 
“Thank you for coming to 
vote today. May I please have 
your VoƟng Pass?” 
2. Review the VoƟng Pass for any 
special messages. If it does 
not have special messages, 
proceed to Step 3. If it does 
have special messages (and no 
strike-out appears), direct 
voter to Help Desk. 
3. Locate the ballot style number 
on the VoƟng Pass, and select 
a ballot with the same 
number. Write the ballot stub 
number and your iniƟals on 
the VoƟng Pass. Write your 
iniƟals on the ballot stub. Do 
not write on the ballot.  
4. Fold the ballot along the 
perforated line and carefully 
detach the ballot from the pad 
and do not tear oﬀ more than one ballot at a Ɵme.   
5. Show the voter that the ballot they are receiving matches the ballot style on the VoƟng Pass. Say to the 
voter: “Please see that I am giving you one ballot with the correct ballot style.” Place the ballot inside 
the white secrecy sleeve and give them to the voter. Say to the voter: “AŌer you've marked you ballot, 
please put your ballot back into the secrecy sleeve.” 
6. Refer to the demonstraƟon ballot on display and say to the voter: “Fill in the oval to make your 
selecƟons. Do not use an X or on your ballot. Make only one choice per contest. You may use any 
available voƟng booth, and then go to the tabulator near the exit to cast your vote.” 
7. File the VoƟng Pass in the EViD Accordion Folder by ﬁrst leƩer of last name.   
 
 
DemonstraƟon ballot EViD Accordion Folder 
 
34 
 
Do not issue a ballot to a voter who presents a Help Desk Ticket at the Ballot StaƟon. 
Help Desk Tickets state clearly, “DO NOT ISSUE BALLOT” at the top of the Ɵcket. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
D. Voter Marks Ballot in Voting Booth 
(SecƟon 101.031, Fla. Stat.)  
The voter should vote in secret and free from any coercion or inƟmidaƟon. The 
voter may use their sample ballot to assist them in marking their selecƟons on 
their ballot. Sample ballots are now green so you can easily disƟnguish them 
from oﬃcial vote-by-mail ballots.  
 
 
E. Voter Casts Ballot at Tabulator 
Before a voter uses the tabulator, they are given a ballot and 
a secrecy sleeve to take to the voƟng booth. AŌer marking 
the ballot, the voter places the voted ballot into the secrecy 
sleeve and takes it to the tabulator to be cast. The message 
screen on the tabulator should read, “Insert Here” to 
indicate it is ready to accept ballots. The DS300 ballot 
inserƟon screen animaƟon shows voters where to insert the 
printed ballot or ballot card. 
As a voter approaches the tabulator with their ballot, say the 
following: “Please remove your ballot from the secrecy 
sleeve and insert your ballot in any direcƟon into the machine.” Remain four to six feet from the tabulator while 
the voter is casƟng a ballot. Do not look at the voter’s ballot.  
When the voter inserts their ballot, say the following: “Please wait a moment unƟl we are sure your ballot has 
been counted.” Wait for the screen that reads, “Thank you for voƟng. Your ballot has been counted.” 
Once the ballot is accepted, say the following: “Thank you for voƟng. Please help yourself to an ‘I Voted’ sƟcker.”  
Help Desk Ticket 
 Sample ballot 
 
35 
 
Collect the secrecy sleeve from voter. Open the secrecy sleeve and place face down to ensure nothing is leŌ 
inside. Periodically return accumulated secrecy sleeves to the Ballot StaƟon and check to be sure nothing was leŌ 
inside.  
The Public Count will start the day at zero and will increase by one aŌer each printed ballot or ballot 
card is accepted into the tabulator. The “Protected Count” on the tabulator screen indicates total 
number of sheets scanned on the DS300 during its lifeƟme and is used for scheduling regular 
maintenance. 
Error Messages at the Tabulator 
The voter should follow the instrucƟons on the screen: 
 If the ballot is rejected, instruct the voter to turn it over and reinsert it into the DS300.  
 If the ballot is sƟll rejected: 
o Instruct the voter to place the ballot inside the white secrecy sleeve. Do not handle the ballot 
yourself. 
o Follow the on-screen instrucƟons to remedy the problem. 
 If the message “Over Voted Contests” displays on the DS300”:  
o Without looking at the ballot, inform the voter 
that the ballot was rejected because they 
made more than one selecƟon in the same 
contest. The vote for that contest will not be 
counted unless they complete a new ballot. 
o Direct the voter to the Help Desk. 
 If the message “Blank Ballot” displays on the DS300: 
o Inform the voter that they did not mark any 
selecƟons on the ballot, so it is an undervoted 
(blank) ballot. 
o Instruct the voter to read the DS300 screen for 
their opƟons. They can either correct the ballot 
or cast it as-is. It is the voter’s choice.  
o If the voter would like to correct the ballot, 
they select “Return”, and the DS300 returns 
their ballot.  
o Direct the voter to the voƟng booth to mark 
their selecƟons, and ask the voter to return to 
the DS300 once ﬁnished. 
o If the voter wishes to cast the ballot as-is, they 
select “Cast Blank Ballot.” 
 For other error messages:  
o Consult with the Manager to solve the indicated problem.  
o If the tabulator will not accept ballots, alert the Manager for assistance. Once the Manager 
unlocks the emergency compartment, calmly instruct the voter to place their ballot in the 
emergency compartment slot. Say to the voter, “Your ballot is being temporarily placed in the 
emergency compartment. You may return at 7:00 p.m. aŌer the polls close to observe the 
ballots being inserted into the tabulator to be counted.” 

 
36 
 
 
The tabulator scans printed ballots and ballot cards the same way. The “Public Count” on the tabulator 
screen provides the total for both ballot types.  
 
Provisional ballots have the “Oﬃce Use Only” box located in the upper right 
corner of the ballot ﬁlled in completely. They are also inside pink secrecy 
sleeves. Never allow a voter with a provisional ballot to insert it into the 
DS300. Instead, direct voters with provisional ballots to the Help Desk, 
where they are placed inside the Provisional Ballot Envelope. 
 
 
F. Voter Flow Inside the Polling Room 
There are things that poll workers can do to ensure a smooth voƟng experience inside the polling room: 
 Direct voters to available voƟng booths if necessary. 
 Ensure ﬁve feet of clearance between the voter at a voƟng booth and those waiƟng in line. 
 Direct voters to the tabulator once they have marked their ballot. 
 Check the voƟng booths for uncast ballots and give them to the Manager.  
 Remove and discard sample ballots, campaign material or other items leŌ behind by a voter. This task is 
primarily designated to the Inspector Tech at the tabulator and should be done when there is not a voter 
at the tabulator. 
 Check that accessible voƟng equipment is kept operaƟonal throughout the day. 
Provide prompt assistance to voters who request assistance in marking their ballot or casƟng their vote. See Voter 
Asks for Help in CasƟng a Ballot or Using Equipment (p. 37). 
  
Provisional review box

Chapter 5 – Voting Situations  
Voters may have needs which can be accommodated by the ElecƟon Board. PosiƟve experiences when voƟng 
build trust in our elecƟons, so always be courteous and helpful.  
A. Challenge to Voter’s Right to Vote   
(SecƟon 101.111, Fla. Stat.)  
A voter’s right to vote may be challenged at the polling place on ElecƟon Day, or prior to ElecƟon Day in which 
case there will be a notaƟon on the precinct register in the electronic poll book. Any registered voter or Poll 
Watcher of a county may challenge the right of a person to vote in that county. Direct the person challenging a 
voter and the voter to the Help Desk. 
 
B. Provisional Ballot 
 (52 U.S.C. SecƟon 21082, SecƟons 101.048, 101.049, 101.111, Fla. Stat.)  
Voters have a right to vote a provisional ballot in certain circumstances. This is a fail-safe mechanism provided 
under both federal and state law. Managers may accommodate this at the Help Desk. 
 
C. Voter Asks for Help in Casting a Ballot or Using Equipment  
(SecƟons 97.061, 101.031(4), 101.051, Fla. Stat.)  
If aŌer entering the voƟng booth, the voter asks for more instrucƟons on how to mark the ballot or use the 
equipment, two poll workers of diﬀerent poliƟcal parƟes, if present, or, if not, two poll workers of same poliƟcal 
party, shall assist the voter. No one assisƟng the voter shall in any manner request, suggest, or seek to persuade 
or induce anyone to vote for or against any parƟcular Ɵcket, candidate, amendment, quesƟon, or proposiƟon. 
AŌer giving the voter instrucƟons, the poll workers shall leave the voter to vote in secret. For more informaƟon 
on assisƟng all voters, see Assistance at the Polls (p. 43). 
 
D. Voter Asks for Another Ballot  
(SecƟon 101.5608(2)(b), Fla. Stat.) 
If a voter makes a mistake on their printed ballot or ballot card, whichever is applicable, the voter may receive 
another ballot or a ballot card, as is applicable, unless the printed ballot or ballot card has been cast in the 
tabulator. The uncast printed ballot or ballot card that has a mistake on it is referred to as a “spoiled ballot.”  
Direct the voter to the Help Desk.  
 
Once the Manager has ﬁled the spoiled ballot, they will escort the voter to the Ballot StaƟon. At this point, the 
Inspector Tech will: 
1. Issue the new ballot by drawing a line through the previous ballot stub number on their VoƟng Pass to 
indicate the ballot was spoiled (i.e., 0454, 0462, 0465). Write the new ballot stub number on the VoƟng 
Pass. File the VoƟng Pass in the EViD Accordion Folder. IniƟal the ballot stub. Tear it from the ballot pad 
and hand it to the voter in a white secrecy sleeve. 
2. Before issuing a third ballot, inform the voter that they will not be issued a fourth ballot if they make a 
mistake on the third ballot. Encourage the voter to use the ExpressVote as it does not allow overvotes. 
3. If three stub numbers have been wriƩen and three ballots are spoiled, the voter may not receive another 
ballot. 
 
38 
 
Do not under any circumstances issue a fourth ballot. Do not under any circumstances issue another 
ballot to a voter that has already cast a ballot into the tabulator! 
 
 
 
Use the same process to indicate that addiƟonal ExpressVote ballot cards were issued (i.e. 2, 3). 
 
 
 
E. Voter Disputes Party Afﬁliation 
If the voter disputes their party aﬃliaƟon as indicated in the EViD, direct the voter to the Help Desk. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
VoƟng Pass – ballot card 
 VoƟng Pass – printed ballot 
 
39 
 
F. Voter Who Requested Vote-by-Mail Ballot Goes to Vote at Polls  
(SecƟon 101.69, Fla. Stat.)  
A voter who has requested a vote-by-mail ballot may go instead to 
vote at the polling place.  
 
If the electronic poll book indicates that a vote-by-mail ballot was 
mailed to the voter: Proceed with check in as usual and issue a 
VoƟng Pass. See VoƟng Process (p. 22). Do not make a voter 
produce their ballot to vote.  
 
However, if an Inspector Tech becomes aware of the vote-by-mail 
ballot (voted or not) because it is visible or because the voter 
indicates that they have it with them, the ballot must be 
surrendered before they can be checked in and issued a new ballot at the Ballot StaƟon. Voters may not bring a 
vote-by-mail ballot into the voƟng booth. 
 
If the voter returns the vote-by-mail ballot, voted or not, follow these procedures to ensure the returned ballot 
cannot be tabulated:   
 Voter should physically alter the envelope containing the ballot in a manner that notches, cuts, or tears 
through the Ɵming marks of the ballot and surrender it to the poll worker.  
 AlternaƟvely, the voter may remove the ballot from the envelope to physically alter it and then place it 
back into the envelope. In this case, once surrendered, the poll worker should write “canceled” on the 
envelope.  
 The voter may choose to have the poll worker do the alteraƟon of the ballot, if they prefer. 
 The poll worker should give the canceled ballot to the Manager or Assistant Manager. 
 The Manager or Assistant Manager will ﬁle the surrendered ballot (and the envelope/secrecy sleeve, if 
any) in the Surrendered VBM Ballot Envelope. 
If the voter requests to use the returned ballot which the voter has marked as a sample ballot, allow the 
voter to take a photo of their ballot before surrendering the ballot. 
 
 
G. Voter Requests to Use Ballot-Marking Device 
If the voter requests to use a ballot-marking device, they cannot check in at the Check-In StaƟon. Instead, direct 
them to the Help Desk to check in and receive their ballot. If the voter was mistakenly issued a VoƟng Pass at the 
Check-In StaƟon instead of the Help Desk, you must undo the check-in before direcƟng them to the Help Desk. 
See Undo Voter Check-In (p. 30).  
 
Anyone can request to use the ballot-marking device. Do not ask quesƟons regarding the health status 
of any voter as this is against the law. Direct the voter to the Help Desk if they wish to use the ballot-
marking device. For instrucƟons on using the ballot-marking device, see Ballot-Marking Device (p. 47). 
 
  

 
40 
 
H. Secure Ballot Intake Stations for Voted Vote-by-Mail Ballots 
(SecƟon 101.69, Fla. Stat.)  
The only place where a voted vote-by-mail ballot may be dropped oﬀ on 
ElecƟon Day is the Duval County Supervisor of ElecƟons Oﬃce at 105 East 
Monroe St. The designated staﬀ members should ensure that prior to the ballot 
being dropped oﬀ that the voter has placed and sealed their voted ballot in the 
envelope and signed the cerƟﬁcate. When the polls close at 7:00 p.m., staﬀ 
must perform ballot accounƟng procedures to account for the ballots in the 
secure ballot intake staƟon.   
 
 
I. Voter Needs to Change Name/Address 
 (SecƟons 101.045, 101.048, Fla. Stat.) 
A voter can change their name or address at the polling place on ElecƟon Day. If 
a voter wishes to update their registraƟon, direct them to the Help Desk to ﬁll 
out a MulƟpurpose Form. 
The MulƟpurpose Form combines four common forms into one convenient form: 
 Florida Voter RegistraƟon ApplicaƟon 
 DeclaraƟon to Secure Assistance  
 DeclaraƟon to Provide Assistance 
 Signature Aﬃdavit 
 
Only one form is needed per voter with the relevant secƟons completed. A voter must only complete the secƟon 
of the form that applies to the voter’s situaƟon.  
Secure Ballot Intake StaƟon

Chapter 6 – Assistance at the Polls  
 
The only Ɵme we may meet our voters in person is when they come to our polling place. We strive to make this a 
posiƟve and memorable experience for all voters. Best pracƟces for all voters include assisƟng without judgement, 
treaƟng all voters equally and making no assumpƟons. When voters are saƟsﬁed with their voƟng process, they 
tend to have more conﬁdence in the elecƟon results and parƟcipate more in future elecƟons.  
 
A. Right to Assistance  
(SecƟon 101.031, Fla. Stat.) 
All voters have the right to ask for and receive help in voƟng from any person of their choosing other than their 
employer, employer’s agent, or union representaƟve; including but not limited to reasons of blindness, disability, 
inability to read or write, or limited-language proﬁciency.  
 
If a voter requests to use a ballot-marking device, do not check them in. Direct the voter to the Help 
Desk to check in and receive their ballot card.  
 
B. Voter Requests Language Assistance  
(52 U.S.C. 10303(f)(4) and 52 U.S.C. 10503(b)(2)(A)) 
Some voters may need language assistance due to an inability to read, write, or speak English or because of 
limited-English language proﬁciency. A voter needing language assistance must be allowed to:  
 Bring along a person to interpret as long as that person is not an employer, an agent of the employer, or 
union representaƟve.  
 Vote using a Spanish translated printed ballot or a ballot-marking device that provides the voter an 
opƟon to view and select contest choices in Spanish and produces a voter-veriﬁable ballot card with the 
voter’s selecƟons for subsequent tabulaƟon.  
 Have available for reading sample ballots, other voƟng and registraƟon materials and informaƟon 
translated in Spanish.  
 Have language assistance provided by bilingual poll worker, professional translator services by phone, 
interacƟve or virtual service for translaƟon, or mulƟ-language-audio-capable voƟng equipment. 
 
A link to the ElecƟon Assistance Commission’s online glossary enƟtled “ElecƟon Phrases at a Glance – English to 
Spanish” is available in the Voter InformaƟon Binder at each polling place. If you are serving as a bilingual poll 
worker, wear idenƟﬁcaƟon in the required language at all Ɵmes while in the polling place.  
 
If a voter requests language assistance, direct the voter to the Help Desk. 
 
C. Voters with Disabilities 
(SecƟons 97.061, 101.051, 101.715, Fla. Stat.; Americans with DisabiliƟes Act - 42 U.S.C ch. 126; VoƟng Accessibility 
for the Elderly and Handicapped Act –52 U.S.C. ch. 201.) 
Overview  
All voters have the right to request and obtain assistance in voƟng. Some voters may have a disability that is not 
visible or evident. Do not make assumpƟons about the needs of any voters. Regardless of the name of the 
disability, the basic steps below should be followed:   
 

 
44 
 
 Treat every voter with the same dignity and respect you would want, expect, and deserve. 
 Be professional, courteous, and paƟent in words, tone, and acƟons. 
 Use “People First” language. For example: “a person who uses a wheelchair” instead of “wheelchair 
bound” or “a person conﬁned to a wheelchair,” and “a person who is blind” instead of “a blind person.”  
 Avoid outdated terms such as “handicapped” or “crippled,” or contrived or potenƟally oﬀensive terms 
such as “diﬀerently abled” or “physically challenged.” 
 Avoid using vague and exclusive group terms such as “they” or “them” for voters with disabiliƟes. 
 Speak directly to the voter, even if accompanied by a companion, caregiver, or interpreter. 
 Treat assisƟve devices as an extension of the voter whether it is a wheelchair, scooter, crutch, walker, 
cane, eye wear, hearing aid, prostheƟc device, or orthoƟc device. Always ask permission before touching 
or grabbing their assisƟve devices. 
 Listen carefully to the voter to learn how to best help the voter. Explain the process and, if needed, give 
the voter more Ɵme to complete the task at hand. 
 Allow the voter with a disability to choose the accommodaƟons the voter requires to vote (magnifying 
sheet, headphones, tacƟle input pad, or assistance from two poll workers). 
 
Cognition  
A voter with a cogniƟve disability may have diﬃculty with memory, aƩenƟon, reading, or sight.  
 Be paƟent and respecƞul. 
 Use a calm demeanor. 
 Use clear and concise words and short sentences to explain the process. 
 Repeat instrucƟons as may be needed for the voter to understand and process what is being said. 
 Give the voter Ɵme to move through the process without feeling rushed. 
 
Hearing  
A voter who is deaf, hard of hearing, or with mild or moderate hearing ability may use a hearing device or an 
interpreter. It may not be immediately obvious.   
 Do not shout as it may make you harder to understand, especially if the voter reads lips. 
 Rephrases, rather than repeat, sentences that the voter may not understand. 
 Speak directly to the voter, even if accompanied by an interpreter and maintain eye contact with the 
voter. 
 
Mobility  
A voter with a permanent or temporary physical disability may use a cane, a crutch, a walker, or a wheelchair to 
move around. The degree of mobility may vary. For example, a voter using a wheelchair may sƟll be able to use 
their hands and arms or get out of the wheelchair and walk a short distance. A person with respiratory or heart 
trouble may not appear to be mobility impaired, but they may need to sit down. Oﬀer a chair with arms, if 
available. 
 Do not lean across a wheelchair user to talk to someone else or to shake another person’s hand. 
 Make eye contact with the voter. If voter is in a wheelchair, be at eye level, if possible, by either siƫng in 
a chair or standing at a slight distance. 
 Do not pull or touch a voter’s wheelchair unless you have received permission to do so. The chair is part 
of the voter’s personal space. 
 Ensure ramps provide the closest accessibility to the polling place. This is important not only for voters 
who may be using wheelchairs but for voters who may be using canes, crutches, or walkers. 
 Ensure ramps are not obstructed so they can be used without diﬃculty.  
 Ensure there is a clear path of travel into and out of the polling place for persons who may be in 
wheelchairs or using other assisƟve devices. 
 
45 
 
 Be aware of the reach limits of people in wheelchairs. If a counter is too high for a wheelchair user to see 
over it, step around it to conduct business with the person. 
 Ask ﬁrst before touching or grabbing a cane, crutch, or walker. People who use them rely on them for 
balance. 
 
Sight 
A voter who is blind or has low vision may use glasses or a magnifying glass, or travel with a service animal 
deﬁned by law, a cane, or a sighted companion, or be alone. Be prepared to oﬀer assistance in orientaƟon and 
reading to anyone who requests it. It is not always obvious when a voter has a visual impairment. 
 A poll worker should idenƟfy themself with name and Ɵtle when approaching a person who is blind or 
has low vision. When deparƟng, let him or her know. 
 Do not speak loudly to a person who is blind or has low vision. 
 If voter asks to be guided, oﬀer an arm or shoulder to guide and walk slightly in front of the voter. 
 Describe the seƫng including any partly open doors, steps, or ramps. 
 Do not touch a person’s cane. If the person puts down the cane in an unsafe place, do not move it. Tell 
the person kindly to move it. That way, the person will know where the cane is. 
 Be speciﬁc with warnings such as stop, swerve, duck, or bend. Do not shout or use non-speciﬁc warnings 
such as “Look out!” 
 Be speciﬁc and non-visual with direcƟons. Do not tell someone to turn leŌ at the end of the desk. It 
would be beƩer to say, “Take ﬁve steps and turn leŌ.” 
 Read informaƟonal signs that appear in print on the walls of the polling place. 
 Oﬀer a signature guide, magniﬁer sheet or lens if requested by the voter. 
 Oﬀer the voter who is blind or has low vision the opƟon of voƟng on the accessible voƟng device. If the 
voter would rather have assistance, allow him or her to be accompanied in the voƟng booth by someone 
of their choosing or two elecƟon oﬃcials. 
 
Speech and Communication  
A voter with a speech or communicaƟon disability may have diﬃculty with speaking, memory, or concentraƟon. 
It may not be immediately obvious.  
 Be paƟent and do not interrupt. Wait for the voter to ﬁnish speaking. 
 If you do not understand or need clariﬁcaƟon, restate what you understood. 
 If needed or requested, provide the voter a pen/pencil and paper to write quesƟons and answers. 
 
 
D. Service Animals 
(Title II of the Americans with DisabiliƟes Act, 42 U.S.C. SecƟons 12131-12134, and SecƟon 413.08 Fla Stat.) 
VoƟng precincts must accommodate voters who are aided by service animals. The provision of emoƟonal support, 
well-being, comfort, and companionship are NOT considered work or tasks for purposes of the deﬁniƟon of a 
service animal. If a voter is using a service animal, walk on the opposite side of where the dog is guiding the voter. 
Do not talk to, distract, or otherwise touch service animals including their harness or leash. The service animal is 
“on duty.” Never ask a voter why they have a service animal. 
  
 
46 
 
Chapter 7 – Voting Systems Operation  
(SecƟons 101.5601-101.5614, 101.56062, 101.5604, 101.56075, Fla. Stat.)  
A. Overview  
Poll workers should bring their Precinct Manual to the polling place with them on ElecƟon Day to be used as a 
reference on how to set up the voƟng systems in the polling place, how to operate the voƟng equipment, how to 
troubleshoot, how to lock the tabulator against further voƟng aŌer the polls have closed, how to properly relay 
vote totals, and how to handle the ballots aŌer the polls have closed.   
 
Although technology is used to conduct and administrate elecƟons, all voƟng in Florida must be by 
paper ballot, whether marked by hand or by using a ballot-marking device that produces a voter-
veriﬁable paper output. Paper ballots are cast into tabulators to be counted. 
 
B. MiFi Hotspot 
The MiFi hotspot is a mobile WiFi hotspot used to establish a wireless connecƟon between the EViDs at a polling 
place and the Duval County Supervisor of ElecƟons data center. It allows a voter’s status to be checked in real 
Ɵme. Polling places at libraries do not use the MiFi hotspot because the EViDs automaƟcally connect to the 
library’s secured wireless internet. 
Opening Procedures 
The Manager will provide the Black Pouch 
containing the MiFi hotspot. To turn on, 
press and hold the round power buƩon 
(follow the red arrow). Check the levels of 
your baƩery and wireless signal. The 
hotspot should be fully charged, but is 
typically plugged into an outlet unless 
wireless signal is low. If wireless signal is 
low, unplug the hotspot and move it to a 
window for improved signal. If MiFi hotspot 
is moved due to low wireless signal and 
unplugged with no outlet nearby, 
periodically check every hour for baƩery 
percentage. If 15% or less, plug back into 
outlet or use an orange extension cord. If 
you need to wake the hotspot display, press 
and release the power buƩon. 
Closing Procedures 
Unplug MiFi hotspot from power outlet. To 
turn oﬀ the MiFi hotspot, press and hold the 
power buƩon (follow the red arrow) for 3 
seconds. Select “OK” to shut down. You will 
see a “MiFi Powering Oﬀ” message and the 
screen will go blank aŌer a few seconds. 
Place the hotspot in the Black Pouch and 
return it to the Manager. 
MiFi hotspot

marking process, eliminaƟng unclear marks and the need for interpretaƟon 
of the voter’s mark.

How To Use 
The ExpressVote printer is used to acƟvate ExpressVote ballot cards at the 
Help Desk. The ExpressVote printer is aƩached to the EViD Edge tablet. If a 
voter wishes to use the ballot-marking device, they must check in at the 
Help Desk. A voter who chooses to mark their ballot using ExpressVote will 
need to have a blank ballot card inserted into the ExpressVote printer to use 
the ExpressVote ballot-marking device. The voter’s ballot style idenƟﬁer will 
be printed onto the blank ballot card and then the voter may proceed to 
the ExpressVote to mark their ballot.  
The following applies when voƟng using the ExpressVote:  
Round barrel key 
ADA table 
Setup complete 
 
49 
 
1. The voter is given a blank ballot card and a secrecy sleeve at the Help Desk to take to the ExpressVote 
booth. If a voter asks for assistance, the poll worker will accompany the voter to the ballot-marking 
device, and will assist as needed with the ExpressVote’s accessibility features (font size, volume control, 
tacƟle keypad, etc.). See Accessibility Features (p. 51).  
2. The voter inserts the ballot card into the ExpressVote face-up with the corner cut edge on the top right. 
Once the display appears on the screen, the voter will be given privacy to vote and mark their ballot. 
 
3. The voter marks the ballot on the touchscreen either by hand, with the stylus, with an audio tacƟle 
keypad and headphones, or by using the voter’s personal sip and puﬀ device by following the wriƩen 
and/or audio instrucƟons: 
 Touch to select the name of the candidate or other contest choice. The system displays a check 
mark and green highlighƟng to indicate the selecƟon.  
 To deselect a contest choice, touch the name or contest again. The system removes the check 
mark and highlighƟng from the selecƟon.  
 The voter will progress through each contest on the ballot by selecƟng “Next Contest.”  
 For contests that allow write-in votes, touch the write-in selecƟon on the contest screen. The 
system displays an on-screen keyboard. Touch the on-screen keyboard to enter the write-in name, 
then touch “Accept.”  
 

 
50 
 
4. The voter has an opportunity to review a summary of all ballot choices made. The voter should select 
“Next Page” if there is more than one page of ballot choices. The voter may be alerted to an error on 
their ballot summary. Ballot-marking devices do not allow a voter to overvote a ballot. When selecƟons 
are being reviewed by the voter, the system will alert the voter that one or more contests have been 
undervoted, giving the voter the opportunity to select a choice if they wish to do so. However, a voter 
can undervote the ballot if desired. To change a ballot selecƟon, select “Return to Ballot” and to make 
new ballot selecƟons. 
5. Once the voter is saƟsﬁed with the selecƟons made, the voter should print the ballot by selecƟng “Print 
Ballot” and then selecƟng “Print” on the conﬁrmaƟon screen. The voter should also review the selecƟons 
on the marked ballot card. If the voter requests another ballot, see Voter Asks for Another Ballot (p. 37). 
6. The voter should cast the marked ballot into the tabulator.

D. Electronic Poll Book (EViD) 
(SecƟons 98.461, 101.5608(1), 101.043(1), 101.045, 101.49, Fla. Stat.) 
The EViD (Electronic Voter iDenƟﬁcaƟon) is an electronic poll book used in place of a 
printed Paper Register to check-in voters, determine whether they have already been 
mailed a vote-by-mail ballot or already voted, update voter informaƟon, and direct 
voters at the wrong locaƟon to their correct polling place. The EViD prints VoƟng 
Passes, AcƟon Required Tickets and Help Desk Tickets. EViDs are used by Inspector 
Techs to check in voters and EViD Edge tablets are used by Managers at the Help Desk.  
Height adjustment lever 
 
54 
 
Opening Checklist 
The Manager will provide the EViD acƟvator. There are two EViDs in each case. 
□ Unlatch and open the black EViD case. 
□ Remove the EViD, power cord and power block, and signature pad from the black EViD case. 
□ Connect the power cord to the power block. 
□ Pull the height adjustment lever, and raise the EViD. 
□ Plug the power cord into the matching port on the back of the EViD. 
□ Plug the other end into a wall outlet or power strip. 
□ Plug the signature pad into an available USB port on the back of the EViD. 
□ Insert the acƟvator into the acƟvator port on the right side of the EViD. 
□ Plug the scanner into an available USB port on the back of the EViD. The acƟvator and the scanner must 
be plugged in prior to powering on the EViD. 
□ Turn on the EViD by pressing the soŌ-touch power buƩon located on the right side of the EViD. 
□ Once the EViD has powered up, verify that the polling place is correct on screen, and then select “Yes.” 
□ Verify that the date and Ɵme are correct, and then select “Yes.” 
□ Next, use the on-screen keyboard to enter your iniƟals, but make sure to leave the ﬁeld for “Code” blank. 
This tells the EViD who is operaƟng the system, and it enables your iniƟals to be printed on each VoƟng 
Pass that you issue. If you step away from the EViD or rotate to another staƟon, select the white human 
ﬁgure icon on the Home screen to remove your iniƟals. 
□ Select “Submit”. An Opening Report automaƟcally prints. Tear it oﬀ from the EViD. 
□ One Inspector Tech should sign the boƩom of the Opening Report. 
□ Give the Opening Report to the Manager, who places it into the front of the EViD Accordion Folder. 
□ Unplug the acƟvator from the USB port, and return it to the Manager. The EViD is now ready to check-in 
voters. 
To clear the keyboard from the screen, select the “X” in the boƩom right corner of the screen. To 
backspace, select the leŌ arrow at the top right of keyboard.  
 
All EViDs delivered to a polling place must be turned on and in use unƟl the polls close and the last 
voter in line at 7:00 p.m. has cast their ballot. 

 
55 
 
Changing EViD Printer Paper  
To remove the printer roll, liŌ the black lever on the top leŌ side of the EViD to pop open the lid. Next, remove 
the used EViD printer roll. Replace it with a new EViD printer roll with the paper feeding up and over the top. If 
you do not insert the paper in the right direcƟon, it will not print properly. Snap the lid closed. Extra rolls are 
located at the Check-In StaƟon. 
How To Use 
To check in a voter on the EViD see Voter at Check-In StaƟon (p. 23). 
Closing Checklist 
The Manager will provide the EViD acƟvator.  
□ Insert the EViD acƟvator into the acƟvator port on the right side of each EViD.  
□ Exit Voter Check In by selecƟng “Exit” at the boƩom of the screen.  
□ The number of Ɵcket totals to print should be pre-ﬁlled to “1.” Select “Exit Program.” A Closing Report will 
print and the EViD will power down automaƟcally. 
□ Place the Closing Report in the front of the EViD Accordion Folder. 
□ Remove acƟvator and return it to the Manager to place in the Black Pouch. 
□ Unplug the scanner and wrap the cord around it. Pack it in the Blue Bag. 
□ Place the system components back in the case carefully and close and lock the case.  
 

 
56 
 
 
The order in which the EViDs are shut down is important. Exit from “Pawn” EViDs before exiƟng the 
“King” EViD (notaƟon on boƩom of screen). 
 
 
 
VoƟng equipment must never be shut down unƟl the last voter in line at 7:00 p.m. has cast their ballot 
and leŌ the polling room. If it is determined that any equipment was shut down before the last voter 
exited the polling room, all poll workers may be terminated. If you see something, say something. NoƟfy 
the Call Center immediately at (904) 255-VOTE [8683]. 
 
Accessibility and Usability 
To change font size or color theme, select “More” on the Home screen, and then select “Accessibility.” 
     
 

 
57 
 
To increase or decrease the font size, select “Set Text Size,” then select the size, and select “OK.” 
      
          
To change the color theme, select “Enable Dark Mode” or “Enable Light Mode” and then select “Yes.” 
       
The EViD will reset back to the default font size and color theme when you log out.

E. Tabulator (DS300) 
(SecƟons 101.5606, 101.5608(2), Fla. Stat.) 
The DS300 is a tabulator used to tally votes as ballots are inserted, transmit results, and print elecƟon reports. 
The DS300 is aƩached to the top of the ballot box, which contains a ballot bin and an emergency compartment.  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
The locks on the DS300 are pick-resistant locks requiring that the key be pushed in while turning to 
unlock. The key does not need to be pushed in while locking. Also, the compartment locks must be in 
the locked posiƟon for the key to be removed.

provide the ﬂat silver key, the ﬂat black key, the Broken Seals Envelope, and the DS300 Tape Envelope. The 
Manager and Assistant Manager complete the Chain of Custody Log while observing the setup. (p. 61).

seal from the Extra Seals Envelope. 
□ Close and lock the empty ballot bin. 
□ Close the ballot box lid and latch the locks and then 
lock the top with the ﬂat silver key.  
□ Unplug and wind the power cord around the hooks 
on the back of the DS300. 
The tabulator, sealed Red Bags, and sealed Blue Bag with Red Handles are now ready for the Manager’s Chain of 
Custody procedures. 
  

 
64 
 
Chapter 8 – Closing the Polls  
(SecƟons 100.011(1), 101.049, 101.5614(1) Fla. Stat.)  
A. Announcement  
At the scheduled closing hour at 7:00 p.m. on ElecƟon Day, the Manager shall announce that the polls 
are closed and that anyone in line will be allowed to vote. The ElecƟon Board must complete steps 
including, but not limited to, securing equipment and ballots. AŌer all voters have ﬁnished voƟng and 
the polls are closed, the proceedings are open to the public.    
 
B. Voters in Line at Close of Polls  
All eligible voters standing in line at the polling place by 7:00 p.m. on ElecƟon Day shall be allowed to vote. The 
Deputy shall stand behind the last person in line to clearly mark the end of the line and establish a cut-oﬀ point. 
Any voters in line aŌer the closing Ɵme must be issued a provisional ballot at the Help Desk. 
 
C. Securing Voting Equipment  
Once all voters in line by close of polls have voted, the ElecƟon Board must secure the voƟng device so that no 
more ballots may be cast. Then, the ElecƟon Board shall complete all ElecƟon Day closing procedures. The public 
is permiƩed to witness the closing of the polls, but no photography is allowed.  
 
D. Closing Procedures 
Technology 
Inspector Techs work with the Manager or Assistant Manager to turn oﬀ and pack up the MiFi hotspot, electronic 
poll book(s), ballot-marking device(s) and the tabulator(s). 
 Turn oﬀ and pack up the MiFi hotspot. For instrucƟons, see MiFi Hotspot (p. 46). 
 Turn oﬀ and pack up the ballot-marking device(s). For instrucƟons, see Ballot-marking Device (p. 47). 
 Turn oﬀ and pack up the electronic poll book. For instrucƟons, see Electronic Poll Book (p. 53). 
 Turn oﬀ and pack up the tabulator(s). For instrucƟons, see Tabulator (p. 58). 
VoƟng equipment must not be shut down unƟl the Manager has announced that polls are closed and the 
last voter has exited the polling room. No equipment may be shut down early for any reason.  
Clean Up 
All poll workers are responsible for cleaning up inside the polling room aŌer the last voter has voted. 
 Return your ID badge to the Manager. 
 Break down all voƟng booths and tables. 
 Put away all precinct equipment, signs and supplies. 
 Clean the polling place and return it to its original condiƟon. 
 Bring back into the polling room all cones, the A-frame sign, and the call bell (if applicable). 
 Retrieve all unused ballots and ballot cards from the Ballot StaƟon/Help Desk and return to Black Bag.  
 Retrieve the box of EViD tape from Check-In StaƟon and leave the box out for the movers.  
 Retrieve the ﬂag and base from the Help Desk and place them in the Sign Kiosk box. 
 PosiƟoning the voƟng systems into the same locaƟons where you found them when you arrived. 
 
You are responsible for the condiƟon of your polling place. The Manager will not dismiss you unƟl the 
polling place has been returned to the way it was found. Do not leave the polling place unƟl dismissed 
by the Manager. Leaving prior to dismissal will result in employee terminaƟon.   

 
65 
 
Chapter 9 – Contingency  
(SecƟons 102.014(5)(k), 102.031, 102.101, 101.733 Fla. Stat.)  
A. Security  
Maintain a heightened awareness for potenƟally threatening acƟvity such as suspicious vehicles, persons, or 
packages near the polling place. Report any suspicious acƟvity to the Manager immediately. 
  
 Carefully monitor loitering in or around the polling place. 
 Be aware of strange or suspicious vehicles parked for prolonged periods of Ɵme near the polling place. 
 Be certain the polling place entrances and exits are clearly marked with signage. Limit voter access points to 
the facility and enforce any building access restricƟons that exist. 
 Encourage all poll workers to be parƟcularly vigilant in prevenƟng voters from accessing areas of the 
building not used for voƟng. 
 Be certain all workers wear proper idenƟﬁcaƟon credenƟals as required by law. 
 Ensure your phone is charged and turned on. 
 IdenƟfy evacuaƟon routes and assembly areas 100-300 feet away from the building. 
 Avoid exiƟng the polling place alone at night. All poll workers should exit the building together aŌer being 
dismissed by the Manager. 
 All alarms should be treated as true emergency noƟﬁcaƟons. Announcements that buildings are safe to re-
occupy are to come from qualiﬁed personnel only. 
 
Active Shooter 
AcƟve shooter situaƟons are unpredictable and evolve quickly. Call or text 911 if you can.  
 

 
66 
 
B. Medical or Emergency Event  
In the event of a medical or other emergency requiring assistance from the police, ﬁre department, or medical 
personnel, call 911 and alert the Manager to report the incident. If the polling room must be evacuated, the 
Manager will announce it and the tabulator must be unplugged and rolled out of the polling room. The tabulator 
should be monitored at all Ɵmes by poll workers. 
C.  Power Outage 
If there is a power outage at the polling place, the Manager will noƟfy the Duval County Supervisor of ElecƟons 
Manager Hotline immediately.  
Paper Register Procedures 
Do not use the paper register procedures to check in voters unless directed by the Manager. The Manager will 
provide the White Bag containing the Paper Register. 
1. Use the heavy paper provided in the Blue Bag to separate the Paper Register and list the alphabet for 
voters to know which check-in table to use (i.e., A-G, H-M, etc.). 
2. Move the ballot pads and white secrecy sleeves from the Ballot StaƟon to the Check-in StaƟon. During 
paper register procedures, ballots are issued at the Check-in StaƟon by the Inspector Tech as voters are 
checked in. 
3. Open the White Bag and begin checking in voters with the Paper Register. Use the register rings to put the 
Paper Register together.

4. Signal the next person in line and say the following: “Welcome! May I please have your photo and 
signature ID?” To verify the idenƟty of the voter, see Voter at Check-in StaƟon (p. 23).  
5. Find the voter’s name on the register. If necessary to ﬁnd the voter’s name, ask voter for date of birth. If 
voter’s name is not on the register or if the voter’s name has changed, direct the voter to the Help Desk.   
6. Check for special messages printed on the register. If there is a special message on the register, the issue 
must be resolved before the voter may sign the register to receive a ballot. 
 If the register indicates “ASSISTANCE REQUIRED,” see Assistance at the Polls (p. 43). 
 If the register indicates “INACTIVE VOTER” or “*Protected*,” you may disregard the notaƟon. 
 If “ABSENTEE REQUESTED” is indicated and the voter is returning the vote-by-mail ballot, ﬁrst mark 
the returned vote-by-mail ballot “Canceled” on the cerƟﬁcate side of the ballot envelope and give the 
ballot to the Manager or Assistant Manager. If the voter does not have the vote-by-mail ballot with 
them to return, direct the voter to the Help Desk to vote a provisional ballot. 
 If “VOTED ABSENTEE,” “VOTED EARLY ,” “VOTED PROVISIONAL,” “Pending: Must Vote Provisional,” 
“Challenged: Must Vote Provisional,” or “PotenƟally Ineligible” is printed in the signature column, 
direct the voter to the Help Desk.  
7. Point to the line where the voter should sign. Do not turn the Paper Register around. The register is 
designed with the signature line facing the voter. The voter signs underneath their name. Compare 
signature with that on voter’s ID. If the signatures do not match, direct the voter to the Help Desk.  
8. Select proper ballot for voter as indicated on the register. Select a ballot with this same code in its upper 
leŌ-hand corner. IniƟal register in box provided and iniƟal ballot stub. Write the stub number in the space 
to the right of the voter’s name underneath the ballot style. 
9. Show the voter that the ballot they are receiving matches the ballot style on the Paper Register. Say to the 
voter: “Please see that I am giving you one ballot with the correct ballot style.” Place the ballot inside 
the secrecy sleeve and give it to the voter. 
10. Direct the voter to voƟng booth to mark the ballot. If the voter requests the use of a ballot-marking 
device, direct the voter to the Help Desk. 
 
68 
 
11. AŌer the voter has marked their ballot, direct them to the tabulator. If there is baƩery backup power 
available to the tabulator, see Voter Casts Ballot at Tabulator (p. 34). If no baƩery backup is available, the 
tabulator’s emergency compartment can be unlocked by the Manager and used. In that case, instruct 
voters to cast their ballots into the emergency compartment. Say to the voter, “Your ballot is being 
temporarily placed in the emergency compartment. You may return at 7:00 p.m. aŌer the polls close to 
observe the ballots being inserted into the tabulator to be counted.” Oﬀer the voter an “I Voted” sƟcker. 
ExpressVote 
For the ExpressVote to power on instantly, it must have power. In a power outage, the baƩery indicator will light 
up red. The poll worker can hold down the power buƩon for 5 seconds to boot up the ExpressVote. The indicator 
will light up green. The ExpressVote will noƟfy the poll worker that it does not have a power source and requires 
immediate assistance. The poll worker can bypass this screen by acknowledging this noƟﬁcaƟon. AŌer starƟng 
baƩery power, advise the Manager to call the Technology Hotline immediately to report the power outage. 
D. Extended Polling Hours 
(SecƟons 101.049, 101.048 Fla. Stat.) 
Extended Polling Hours are a rare occurrence, but it is important to be prepared for this situaƟon if it should 
occur. Procedures for Extended Polling Hours are very diﬀerent from regular polling hours. Any person voƟng 
aŌer the last person who was in line during the scheduled closing hour of 7:00 p.m. on ElecƟon Day must vote a 
provisional ballot. These provisional ballots must be kept separate from all other ballots cast during regular 
voƟng hours. Because all voters need to be issued provisional ballots during Extended Polling Hours, Inspector 
Techs will be permiƩed to issue provisional ballots rather than send voters to the Help Desk. The Manager will 
convert all EViDs over to the Extended Hours seƫng.    
 
At Close of Regular Polling Hours at 7:00 p.m. 
The Manager will provide the Deputy with a pink Provisional Ballot Envelope from the Help Desk. The Deputy will 
give the Provisional Ballot Envelope to the ﬁrst person in line aŌer the regular close of polls at 7:00 p.m. This voter 
does not need to do anything with the envelope. The envelope indicates that this voter will be the ﬁrst voter of 
Extended Polling Hours. The Deputy will stand behind the last voter who is in line waiƟng to vote for regular polling 
hours. The Deputy will remain behind this voter unƟl the voter checks in to vote.  
 
After Last Voter in Line During Regular Polling Hours Casts Ballot 
AŌer the last voter in line at the close of regular polling hours at 7:00 p.m. casts a ballot, the Assistant Manager 
and one Inspector tech will close polls on the DS300. See Closing Checklist (p. 62). Complete all the steps. 
 
Distribution of Supplies for Extended Polling Hours 
The Manager will distribute supplies for Extended Polling Hours: 
 Provisional Ballot Envelopes will be placed at the Check-In StaƟon. 
 Pink secrecy sleeves will be placed at the Ballot StaƟon and the White Secrecy Sleeves will be removed.  
 A Green Bag will be posiƟoned near the tabulator. An Inspector Tech will stand beside the bag at all 
Ɵmes. Voters will cast their Provisional Ballot Envelope into this bag. This bag should never be 
unaƩended! 
 One Inspector Tech should pass out clipboards loaded with the Provisional Ballot Envelopes and pens to 
the next few voters in line and explain what should be completed on the envelope.  
 The Manager will be at the Help Desk and the Assistant Manager will be at the Ballot StaƟon. 
 
 
69 
 
At Check-In Station During Extended Polling Hours 
Pick up a Provisional Ballot Envelope and say to the Extended Polling Hours voter, “You are voƟng under 
Extended Polling Hours provisions in FL law. AŌer you mark your ballot, you will put it into this Provisional 
Ballot Envelope and seal it. You will tear oﬀ the top ﬂap which has a tracking number so you can track the 
status of your ballot on our website.” Do not give the envelope to the voter yet. 
 
Voter Search on EViD During Extended Polling Hours 
Before you begin checking in voters, you should see a new Home 
screen much like the regular polling hours Home screen, but 
make sure your EViD shows “Extended Hours” at the very boƩom 
of the screen. If you do not see “Extended Hours” on your EViD 
screen, consult with the Manager before checking in any voters.  
 
Follow procedures for Voter Search at the Check-in StaƟon (p. 25).   
AŌer you ﬁnd the voter, verify that the voter’s name and address 
are correct and select “Yes.” On the Provisional Ballot Entry 
screen, you will enter the tracking number found on the ﬂap of 
the Provisional Ballot Envelope or on the front. Enter the same number into both ﬁelds and select “OK.”

The voter will sign the signature pad and you will verify the signature matches the ID provided, return the ID, and 
issue a VoƟng Pass. 
If you encounter any situaƟon in which you would have directed a voter to the Help Desk during regular 
polling hours, the same procedures apply. Do not check in the voter. Print the Help Desk Ticket when 
prompted and direct the voter to the Help Desk. 
Issuing the Provisional Ballot Envelope 
Before you direct the voter to the Ballot StaƟon, you will need to mark the Provisional Ballot Envelope. The top 
porƟon of the front of the envelope is for the voter’s informaƟon. Since you have already checked in the voter on 
the EViD, the voter does not need to complete or sign this secƟon.  
Front of the Envelope: Fill out the Witness of Help Desk Oﬃcial completely. Sign on the line for “Help Desk Oﬃcial 
Signature” and print your name underneath. Although there is a space for the Manager to sign, only one 
signature is required by law. 
 
Back of the Envelope: Check the box for “Qualiﬁed Voter” located in the 
Reason for Provisional Ballot box. Do not mark anything else.  
 
Tear oﬀ the VoƟng Pass from the EViD printer. Give the VoƟng Pass and the 
Provisional Ballot Envelope to the voter. Direct the voter to the Ballot 
StaƟon. 
At the Ballot Station During Extended Polling Hours 
Follow the procedures for Eligible Voter at Ballot StaƟon (p. 33). Before 
issuing a printed ballot or ballot card, ﬁll in completely the box in the upper 
right corner of the ballot where “Oﬃce Use Only” is indicated. Filling in this 
box will prevent the ballot from being mistakenly cast in the tabulator.  
During Extended Polling Hours, you will use the Pink Secrecy Sleeves. Direct 
the voter to the voƟng booths and say to the voter, “Once you have marked 
your ballot, place it back into the secrecy sleeve and slide BOTH the 
secrecy sleeve and the ballot into the pink envelope and seal it. You may 
tear oﬀ the ﬂap to track your ballot on our website.”  
 Provisional review box 
 
71 
 
Casting a Ballot During Extended Polling Hours 
AŌer checking to see that the voter has sealed the Provisional Ballot Envelope and carefully removed the tracking 
number ﬂap, the Inspector Tech posiƟoned near the tabulator beside the Green Bag will say to the voter, “Please 
cast your ballot into the Green Bag. Thank you for voƟng!” 
Closing Procedures for Extended Polling Hours 
Complete all other closing procedures found in Closing the Polls (p. 64).
