Glossary 
 
A-Frame Sign – A large, white, plasƟc sign used to alert voters near the footpath that approaches the polling room 
entrance.  
Americans with DisabiliƟes Act (ADA) – A federal law that sets standards for ensuring that Americans with 
disabiliƟes have equal access to public services and faciliƟes.  
Assisted Voter – A voter who requests assistance in marking their ballot due to a disability or illiteracy. Assisted 
voters may have a person of their own choice assist them or have two poll workers assist them.  
Audio TacƟle Interface – An assisƟve device or handheld keypad that enables voters with visual, cogniƟve, or 
mobility impairments to mark a ballot. 
Ballot – The oﬃcial paper, with ovals ﬁlled-in by the voter, that contains all the contests to be voted on in a 
speciﬁc precinct. See also “Ballot Card.” 
Ballot Bin– The locked compartment at the boƩom of the tabulator that contains the ballots aŌer they have been 
scanned.  
Ballot Card – The oﬃcial voter-veriﬁable paper output of a ballot-marking device used by the voter, that contains 
all the contests to be voted on in a speciﬁc precinct.  
Ballot-Marking Device – The touch-screen technology that produces a paper record for vote tabulaƟon. Provides 
all voters with a simple and inclusive method of marking their ballot that conforms to ADA guidelines. 
Ballot Style – The type of ballot issued to a voter that is predetermined by the Duval County Supervisor of 
ElecƟons according to the voter’s registraƟon record (voƟng precinct and party aﬃliaƟon).  
Ballot Report Form – The form used to account for the number of ballots received, unscanned, spoiled, 
provisional, voted (cast), used, and unused at each polling place. This form is completed by the Manager and/or 
the Assistant Manager.      
Challenged Voter – A voter whose eligibility to vote has been quesƟoned. Challenged voters may vote a 
provisional ballot.  
Deputy – An poll worker, depuƟzed by the Sheriﬀ, staƟoned outside of the polling room who maintains order at 
the polls and monitors the No SolicitaƟon Zone. 
Drop Zone – The site where designated elecƟon oﬃcials will drop oﬀ red voted ballot bags and precinct supplies 
aŌer the polls close. 
DS300 – See “Tabulator.” 
Early VoƟng – As dictated by Florida elecƟon laws, a period of 8-14 days when voters can vote in-person at any 
Early VoƟng site in the county where they are registered to vote.  
 
73 
 
ElecƟon Board – The Managers and Inspector Techs appointed to conduct an elecƟon. In all quesƟons that may 
arise before members of an ElecƟon Board, the quesƟons are required to be decided by a majority vote. 
ElecƟon Team – All poll workers, including the Deputy, that work at a given precinct during an elecƟon.  
ElecƟons Center – The locaƟon where training classes are held, elecƟon equipment is stored, and precinct 
supplies are delivered and picked up prior to ElecƟon Day. 
Electronic Poll Book – Device that parƟally automates the process of checking in voters, assigning them the 
correct ballot style, and marking voters who have been issued a ballot. May be used in place of a tradiƟonal paper 
poll book or precinct register.  
Electronic Voter IdenƟﬁcaƟon (EViD) – See “Electronic Poll Book.”  
Emergency Compartment – A locked compartment at the front of the tabulator where voters insert ballots that 
cannot be scanned due to equipment malfuncƟon.  
EViD AcƟvator – The USB drive that allows an EViD to access the Florida Voter RegistraƟon database and check-in 
voters. 
ExpressVote – See “Ballot-Marking Device.” 
Florida Statutes – Enacted by the Florida Legislature, all the laws of the State of Florida, including those that 
establish the electoral process in Florida. 
General ElecƟon – An elecƟon held on the ﬁrst Tuesday aŌer the ﬁrst Monday in November in the even-
numbered years for both, the purpose of ﬁlling federal, state, and county oﬃces and for the purpose of voƟng on 
consƟtuƟonal amendments not otherwise provided by law. 
Inspector Tech – A poll worker trained to check in voters using the electronic poll book, issue ballots at the Ballot 
StaƟon and operate the tabulator. 
Manager (Assistant Manager) – The person who oversees a polling place. Referred to as a Clerk in the Florida 
Statutes and administraƟve rules. 
MiFi Hotspot– A mobile Wi-Fi hotspot used to establish a wireless connecƟon between the electronic poll books 
at a precinct and the Duval County Supervisor of ElecƟons data center allowing a voter’s status to be checked in 
real Ɵme.  
MulƟpurpose Form – Form that combines the Florida Voter RegistraƟon ApplicaƟon, DeclaraƟon to Secure 
Assistance, DeclaraƟon to Provide Assistance and Signature Aﬃdavit into one convenient form. 
No SolicitaƟon Zone – As dictated by Florida elecƟon law, the area within a 150’ radius from the entrance of the 
polling place, and within the polling room, where the solicitaƟon of voters is illegal.  
Oath of Person Entering a Challenge – Form provided to voters or Poll Watchers who challenge another voter’s 
right to vote. This form must be completed by the person challenging a voter, and the challenged voter must vote 
a provisional ballot. 
 
74 
 
Overvote – When a voter marks the ballot for more selecƟons in a contest than the number of vacancies to be 
ﬁlled.   
Poll Watcher – Person designated by a poliƟcal party, candidate, or poliƟcal commiƩee to observe the voter 
check-in process and the conduct of the elecƟon.  All Poll Watchers must sign up by the deadline, be approved by 
the Supervisor of ElecƟons, and meet the requirements as provided by the Florida Statutes. 
Poll Worker – Trained individual who processes voters, provides instrucƟons on the proper use of voƟng 
equipment, and solves potenƟal problems that voters encounter at ElecƟon Day polling places. 
Polling Place – The locaƟon and building where voƟng occurs in each precinct. 
Polling Room – The room where voter check-in and the casƟng of ballots take place.  
Precinct – The geographical area that designates where voters go to vote and which ballot they receive.  
Precinct Register – The paper list of the names and addresses of every ciƟzen who is registered to vote in a 
speciﬁc precinct.  
Primary ElecƟon – The nominaƟon of candidates of poliƟcal parƟes as provided by SecƟon 100.061, Florida 
Statutes. Held the Tuesday ten weeks prior to the general elecƟon. 
Provisional Ballot – A ballot cast by an individual whose eligibility to vote cannot be determined.  The validity of 
the provisional ballot is determined by the Canvassing Board provided by SecƟon 101.048, Florida Statutes. 
Results Tape – The tape that is printed on the tabulator aŌer the polls are closed that summarizes the elecƟon 
results for a precinct. 
Rovers – Field service technicians directed by SOE staﬀ to polling places to assist with technical issues.  
Sample Ballot – Paper or digital examples of each precinct’s ballots that are mailed, emailed, or distributed at SOE 
oﬃces that a voter can use to make informed selecƟons prior to voƟng their actual ballot on ElecƟon Day. Sample 
ballots are now green so you can easily disƟnguish them from oﬃcial vote-by-mail ballots. 
Secrecy Sleeve – Issued by the Inspector Tech at the Ballot StaƟon to provide privacy for the ballot. 
Service Animal – A specially-trained animal performing work or tasks to assist persons with a disability. EmoƟonal 
support animals are not the same as service animals according to Florida statute.  
Sign Kiosk – A tri-fold informaƟon board located near the Help Desk that includes informaƟon, noƟces and voter 
instrucƟons in English and Spanish. 
Solicitors – Candidate and/or campaign supporters; peƟƟoners; and news media. Allowed only outside the 150-
foot radius of the No SolicitaƟon Zone. 
Spoiled Ballot – A ballot that has been marked in error, torn, or damaged during the elecƟon, or a ballot that has 
been rejected by the tabulator. Placed in a Spoiled Ballot Envelope and accounted for at the end of the night on 
the Ballot Report Form.    
 
75 
 
Tabulator – VoƟng machine used in Duval County to tally votes, transmit results, and print elecƟon reports on 
ElecƟon Day. 
Undervote – When a voter does not make any selecƟons or makes fewer selecƟons than allowed in a contest on 
the ballot.  
Universal Primary Contest – If all candidates for an oﬃce have the same party aﬃliaƟon and the winner will have 
no opposiƟon in the General ElecƟon, all qualiﬁed voters, regardless of party aﬃliaƟon, may vote in the Primary 
ElecƟon for that oﬃce.  
Unscanned Ballot – A blank or marked ballot intended to be tabulated that was not scanned by the tabulator at 
the end of the elecƟon and will be reviewed by the Canvassing Board. 
Visual Aid Packing Chart – Color-coded chart idenƟfying the locaƟon of all precinct supplies and how to pack the 
backs during closing procedures. 
Vote-by-Mail (formerly known as absentee ballot) - A ballot that is mailed or picked up in advance of an elecƟon. 
Provides the voter the opƟon to vote without having to visit an early voƟng site or polling place on ElecƟon Day. 
Requested by any registered and qualiﬁed voter. 
Voter Accessibility Kit – Contains signature guides, sanitary ear and head covers, and other accessibility 
resources.  
Voter InformaƟon Card (formerly known as Voter RegistraƟon Card) – A card mailed to each registered Florida 
voter lisƟng voter’s name, date of birth, precinct number, party aﬃliaƟon, voter registraƟon number, date of 
issue, districts represenƟng the voter, and polling place. The card is issued for informaƟonal purposes only. 
Voter RegistraƟon Form – A Voter RegistraƟon ApplicaƟon, also known as FVRA form (Florida Voter RegistraƟon 
ApplicaƟon). This is used when a voter wishes to register to vote or to make a change to their voter registraƟon. It 
is found in SecƟon 1 of the MulƟpurpose Form.  
VoƟng Booth – A piece of equipment set-up for voters to mark their ballot in private.  
VoƟng Pass – A Ɵcket given to a voter at check in that authorizes them to receive a ballot.  
Write-in Candidate – A candidate who is qualiﬁed to run for elecƟon by way of the voter wriƟng the name of the 
candidate on the ballot.   
Zero Tape – ElecƟon tape that is printed by the tabulator and veriﬁed by the Manager and the Assistant Manager 
or Inspector Tech before voƟng begins to cerƟfy that all races have a zero total.

Chapter 1 – Introduction 
(SecƟons 102.031, 102.012, 102.014, Fla. Stat.) 
A Manager is responsible for the management of the polling place and poll workers. A Manager (or Assistant 
Manager) is staƟoned at the Help Desk to process provisional ballots, resolve voter challenges, address voƟng 
situaƟons and check in voters when there are no special situaƟons to address. A Manager has the added 
responsibility of transporƟng secure elecƟon supplies, contacƟng poll workers for Pre-ElecƟon Setup, seƫng up 
and taking down the polling room, and making sure that all procedures are followed correctly. A Manager or 
Assistant Manager will relieve poll workers when they go on break. 
 
A. Overview 
Here is an overview of acƟon items for Managers. 
 
 
Review Manager Packet- Call facility to schedule visit to polling place and to schedule Pre-Election Setup- Call all staff to confirm their assignments and confirm who will attend Pre-Election Setup- Visit your polling place no later than 4:00 p.m. on Friday before election to check accessAttend Manager's Pickup- Bring your picture ID- Pick up Yellow Bag- Verify contents of Yellow Bag and ask questionsHost Pre-Election Setup- Verify equipment delivery- Read special instructions- Set up polling room- Communicate reminders to staffOpening Procedures- Call Manager Hotline to report you were able to get into facility - Handle payroll, oath, ID badges- Conduct safety briefing- Set up inside/outside polling room- Login to EViD Edge in Clerk Mode- Set Ready for Voters status on EViD Edge at Help Desk- Announce opening of pollsAt the Help Desk- Operate EViD Edge- Update voter registrations- Issue provisional ballots- Handle special situations- Complete Trouble LogClosing Procedures- Announce closing of the polls- Transmit election results- Complete ballot reconciliation- Clean up polling place- Complete Manager's Drop Zone Checklist- Dismiss poll workers- Deliver bags to Drop Zone
 
8 
B. Training Manuals 
(SecƟon 102.014, Fla. Stat.) 
The Handbook for Precinct Managers details all the procedures and 
documents that are the responsibility of a Manager on ElecƟon Day. The 
Precinct Manual for Poll Oﬃcials details the laws and procedures that must 
be followed by all poll workers at every polling place on ElecƟon Day. In many 
cases, the Handbook for Precinct Managers will reference the Precinct 
Manual for Poll Oﬃcials for speciﬁc procedures and then provide addiƟonal 
informaƟon relevant to the role of a Manager. 
 
All poll workers are expected to familiarize themselves with the manuals in 
the Ɵme between their training and their work assignment. Poll workers are 
required to bring their manuals with them on ElecƟon Day.  
 
C. Quick Reference 
The various bags referenced in this manual will be referred to by the naming convenƟons below. For a complete 
list of all bags and their contents, please see Visual Aid Packing Chart (p. 17). 
Yellow Bag EssenƟal Items 
Orange Bag Voted Provisional Ballots 
Teal Bag Ballot Report Form (pracƟce and ﬁnal) 
Gray Bag Unscanned Ballots 
Black Pouch Memory SƟck/AcƟvator/Hotspot 
White Bag Paper Register 
AdministraƟve Folder Manager’s Resources 
 
Red Bag Voted Ballots 
 
Blue Bag with Red Handles Ballot Stubs and ParƟals 
 
Purple Bag AdministraƟve Supplies 
Brown Form Folder English Forms 
Clear Form Folder Spanish Forms 
 
Blue Bag Precinct Supplies 
Blue Pouch Supplies 
 
Green Bag Extended Hours Only 
 
Black Bag Unused Ballots/Ballot Cards 
 
The AdministraƟve Folder starts in the Yellow Bag. On ElecƟon Day, the AdministraƟve Folder is moved 
to the Help Desk and returned in the Purple Bag. See Unpacking the Bags (p. 20).

Chapter 2 – Before Election Day 
 
A. Stafﬁng Assignments 
Poll Worker Services will mail you a Manager Packet which includes your precinct assignment, a Staﬀ Roster with 
contact informaƟon, and a Blue Polling Place Sheet with assigned polling place contact informaƟon. Expect this 
to arrive by USPS approximately three weeks prior to ElecƟon Day. An updated Staﬀ Roster will be emailed the 
week before ElecƟon Day. 
 
□ Communicate with the polling place contact person to: 
o Schedule a visit to your polling place before 4:00 p.m. on Friday before the elecƟon. 
o Schedule Pre-ElecƟon Setup for Monday before the elecƟon (if your facility will allow it).  
o Verify who will open the polling place on ElecƟon Day morning at 6:00 a.m. and obtain a cell 
and/or home phone number of that person. Do not assume that the contact person you meet 
for your site visit or for Pre-ElecƟon Setup is the same contact person for ElecƟon Day. 
AlternaƟvely, the polling place contact person may provide you with a key and an alarm code. 
 
If the facility contact person or phone numbers are not the same as those listed on the Blue Polling 
Place Sheet, call the Manager Hotline with the update. 
 
□ Contact your staﬀ within 48 hours of receiving your Staﬀ Roster to reconﬁrm that (1) they will be 
working on ElecƟon Day and (2) they know their precinct number and polling place. Remind poll workers 
that arrival Ɵme on ElecƟon Day is 6:00 a.m. sharp.  
 
□ If anyone will not be available to work on ElecƟon Day, email Poll Worker Services at 
DuvalElecƟonsEdu@coj.net as soon as possible so that a subsƟtute may be found. If you are not noƟﬁed 
of a subsƟtute within 48 hours of the elecƟon, call the Manager Hotline. 
 
□ Invite three poll workers to join you for Pre-ElecƟon Setup. Make them aware of the addiƟonal $25 for 
their service and provide them with the date/Ɵme. While parƟcipaƟng in Pre-ElecƟon Setup is not 
required of poll workers, it makes ElecƟon Day morning easier for everyone.  
 
B. Visit to Polling Place 
Managers are required to visit the polling place no later than 4:00 p.m. on Friday before the elecƟon. Check the 
Blue Polling Place Sheet for the date of equipment delivery to your polling place. Most elecƟon equipment is 
delivered to precincts by Friday before the elecƟon. However, if the delivery of equipment to your precinct will 
be on Monday before the elecƟon, then you do not need to visit your polling place unƟl you meet for Pre-
ElecƟon Setup on Monday. 
□ Ensure that you know how to open the polling place and that your key (if provided) works. Some 
locaƟons may also provide you with an alarm code. Some locaƟons have their own representaƟves open 
the building for you on ElecƟon Day. 
□ IdenƟfy the polling room and where to locate your equipment. Do not change the polling room to a 
diﬀerent room in the building. If there are any facility issues or if any items are missing, call the Manager 
Hotline immediately during business hours or email Poll Worker Services.  
□ Verify that the voƟng systems have been delivered to the correct precinct, if already delivered.

C. Manager’s Pickup  
On Monday before the elecƟons, pick up your Yellow Bag at the ElecƟons Center or your selected remote pickup 
site. Only Managers or Assistant Managers may pick up the Yellow Bag.  
□ Follow the direcƟonal signs and cones. Provide a picture ID to the ElecƟons Center staﬀ. You will be given 
the Yellow Bag.  
□ Before leaving Manager’s Pickup, verify that these items are in your Yellow Bag: 
o Blue Polling Place Sheet (facility contact list, key 
status/alarm code, voƟng systems codes, ADA 
informaƟon, Drop Zone assignment and Rover 
contact informaƟon) 
o Facility Key, if applicable  
o Black Pouch containing two EViD acƟvators 
□ Take your Yellow Bag inside your home. Do not leave elecƟon 
materials in your vehicle.  
□ If unsure about anything – park and see the Warehouse 
Manager or Supervisor of ElecƟons staﬀ. 
 
D. Pre-Election Setup 
(SecƟon 102.031, Fla. Stat.) 
Bring your Yellow Bag with you to Pre-ElecƟon Setup on Monday before the elecƟon. NoƟfy the Manager Hotline 
immediately if your equipment has not been delivered, keys do not work, or if your bags are missing items. 
 
Pre-Election Setup Checklist 
□ Verify delivery of elecƟon equipment and conﬁrm that the precinct number on the label matches your 
precinct assignment: 
o 5 Bags Ɵed together (Purple, Blue, 
Red, Blue with Red Handles, Green) 
o EViD Compacts in black cases 
o EVID Edge in gray hard case 
o DS300(s) in free standing black case(s)  
o ExpressVote in soŌ black case  
o ExpressVote printer in black laptop case 
o Black Bag 
□ Conﬁrm that the following addiƟonal equipment has been delivered: 
o VoƟng booths in silver cases  
o ADA booth  
o ADA table  
o A-frame box (signs and A-frame 
inside)  
o Sign Kiosk 
o Orange cones (large and small) 
o Chairs (only certain precincts) 
o Ramps (only certain precincts) 
o ADA orange cones (only certain precincts) 
o Call bell (only certain precincts)  
□ Become familiar with your supplies using the Visual Aid Packing Chart found in the AdministraƟve Folder 
or on p. 17.  
□ Read and follow the direcƟves listed on the Manager’s Special InstrucƟons found in the AdministraƟve 
Folder. If indicated on Manager’s Special InstrucƟons, retrieve Candidate Withdrawal NoƟces from the 
Purple Bag. AƩach the noƟces (English and Spanish) inside every voƟng booth and on the ADA table 
using tape. Do not aƩach the noƟces to any other equipment or signs. 
Yellow Bag 
 
 
13 
□ Familiarize yourself with the building in preparaƟon for your Emergency Safety Procedures Brieﬁng on 
ElecƟon Day. Determine emergency exit route and emergency meeƟng area. See Safety Brieﬁng (p. 15). 
□ Direct poll workers to set up the polling room. See Precinct Manual for Poll Oﬃcials, Set-up Inside the 
Polling Room (Ch. 2).  
□ Lock up the facility when you leave and take the Yellow Bag back with you to your home. 
□ Locate your Drop Zone assignment found on the Blue Polling Place Sheet. Consider driving from the 
polling place to the Drop Zone locaƟon if unfamiliar with the route.  
If desired, you may start up your MiFi hotspot, EViDs and ExpressVote to check connecƟvity. See 
Precinct Manual for Poll Oﬃcials, VoƟng Systems OperaƟon (Ch. 7). Place the Opening Reports in the 
EViD Accordion Folder. While you may leave them plugged in, you must shut them down and remove 
the EViD acƟvator and return it to the Black Pouch in the Yellow Bag.  
 
Do not break any seals during Pre-ElecƟon Setup. The only Ɵme you may break seals is on ElecƟon Day.

Chapter 3 – Opening Procedures 
(SecƟons 102.012, 102.031, 101.5610, Fla. Stat.) 
There are two very important steps to take when arriving at your polling place. The ﬁrst thing you should do on 
ElecƟon Day is call the Manager Hotline to Report In. This means that you have been able to get into your 
facility. Once you are in, your technology is set up, and you are ready to serve voters you will Report On. See 
Announcing the Opening of the Polls (p. 22) for more on that step.   
It is important to know that the public and the media are allowed to witness the assembling of the precinct 
before the polls open, but photography is not allowed. The Manager should designate an area for public 
observaƟon as the public is not permiƩed to wander around the polling room.  
A. Arriving at the Polling Place 
Retrieve the Payroll Envelope from the Yellow Bag. The Payroll Sheet is inside 
the envelope. 
 
Payroll 
Have all poll workers sign the Payroll Sheet. They must verify their address 
on the Payroll Sheet before signing. The address we have on ﬁle is the 
mailing address we use to issue the paycheck. Record on the Payroll Sheet 
the four poll workers who aƩended Pre-ElecƟon Setup. If an assigned poll 
worker does not show up by 6:00 a.m., call the Manager Hotline so an 
alternate poll worker can be assigned. Record on the Payroll Sheet that the 
poll worker did not show up and the reason why, if known.  
 
ID Badges  
Retrieve the ID badges from the Yellow Bag. All poll workers must present a 
valid photo ID. Verify that the photo ID of each Board Member matches the 
ID badge. ID badges are to be worn for the enƟre ElecƟon Day.  
Oath of Election 
Administer the oath of elecƟon to all poll workers (except the Deputy who 
was sworn in during training). The Assistant Manager administers the oath to 
the Manager.  
 
Alternate Poll Workers 
If an alternate poll worker is assigned, they should take the oath upon arrival. Verify that their photo ID matches 
their ID badge. Add them to the Payroll Sheet. 
 
The Manager and Assistant Manager sign the Payroll Sheet. The Payroll Sheet includes acknowledgement of the 
Oath of ElecƟon and ID check. Any unsigned signature line due to poll worker absence should have an “x” 
marked through it. Place the Payroll Sheet in the Payroll Envelope and then return the envelope to the Yellow 
Bag.  
 
Two relaƟves should not sign the Payroll Sheet or any forms that require two ElecƟon Board Members 
to sign. When necessary, a non-relaƟve ElecƟon Board Member should parƟcipate and sign the form. 

 
 
15 
 
B. Safety Brieﬁng 
It is important that all poll workers are aware of what to do in the event of an emergency, such as a bomb threat; 
shooƟng; power outage; or ﬁre, ﬂood, or other natural disaster. Prior to ElecƟon Day, the Manager will idenƟfy 
the emergency exit route from the polling room and will designate a meeƟng area outside the building. Prior to 
opening the polls, the Manager conducts the emergency and safety procedures brieﬁng. For addiƟonal safety 
informaƟon, see Precinct Manual for Poll Oﬃcials, ConƟngency (Ch. 9). 
1. Provide poll workers with the name of the facility where the polling place is located.  
2. Advise poll workers that if they hear a ﬁre alarm and/or if the Manager announces that an evacuaƟon is 
necessary, they should exit the polling room. 
3. Give speciﬁc direcƟons on how to exit the building, such as: “Turn leŌ, walk about 10 yards and look to 
your leŌ where you will see a door marked FIRE EXIT.” 
4. Give the poll workers direcƟons to the locaƟon of the pre-designated meeƟng area outside the building. 
5. The Assistant Manager calls 911 in case of an emergency requiring assistance from the police, ﬁre 
department, or medical personnel. The Assistant Manager must have possession of the wriƩen address 
of the polling place and a cell phone. The Assistant Manager should direct emergency personnel when 
they arrive. AŌer the Assistant Manager calls 911, they should immediately call the Manager Hotline to 
report the emergency. 
6. Assign a poll worker to oversee assisƟng voters in exiƟng the building safely and quickly. 
7. Ask if any of the poll workers have special health or medical condiƟons that the group needs to be aware 
of in case of an emergency. 
8. Remind poll workers that in the event of an acƟve shooter, they should “Avoid, Deny, Defend.” Share any 
speciﬁc details about your facility that may be relevant (i.e. inaccessible rooms, addiƟonal exits, 
situaƟonal awareness). 
Manager    Announce calmly that the polling room must be evacuated. Retrieve the 
Staﬀ Roster (if the situaƟon is not life-threatening). Leave the polling room 
aŌer all poll workers have exited. Once outside, take roll to account for staﬀ. 
Follow instrucƟons given by emergency personnel. 
Assistant Manager Call 911 and provide emergency personnel with address of polling place. 
Inspector Techs If the situaƟon is not life threatening, unplug the tabulator but leave the 
screen up. Carefully roll the tabulator to the designated meeƟng area. 
Deputy Ensure everyone exits in a safe and orderly manner. 
 
Do not re-enter the building unƟl directed to do so by emergency personnel or law enforcement.  
 
 
General Polling Place Security 
 Make sure that your cell phone is fully charged and turned on for ElecƟon Day. Bring your charger. 
 ConƟnually monitor entrances and exits to ensure that only authorized persons are in the polling room 
and any surrounding restricted areas.  
 Prevent unauthorized access to equipment, elecƟon materials and sensiƟve and/or conﬁdenƟal 
documents including signed forms, voted ballots, ballot stock and provisional ballots. 
 Follow established procedures for securing, counƟng, and transporƟng ballots. 

 
16 
 Use the Trouble Log to document procedures, incidents, and troubleshooƟng.  
 Ensure electronic transmission of results, data, and ﬁles are secure. For example, if the signal is weak, 
exercise cauƟon if moving equipment to window or door within public view or access.  
 Review Chain of Custody procedures.  
 Review your emergency procedures for responding to a threat to public safety. 
 Do not allow a sheriﬀ, Deputy, police oﬃcer or other oﬃcer of the law to access a polling room unƟl the 
Manager or majority of the Inspector Techs grant permission or unless the member of law enforcement 
is there to vote. When a Deputy is allowed inside the polling room in an oﬃcial capacity, a Deputy is 
subject to the Manager’s or Inspector Tech’s commands.  
 Reinforce the Deputy’s instrucƟons if there are disrupƟons outside the polling place. You may also call 
the Manager Hotline for addiƟonal assistance. If the situaƟon is life threatening, call 911. 
 
C. Set Up the Inside Polling Room 
If your poll workers did not meet for Pre-ElecƟon Setup on Monday before the elecƟon, ensure that poll workers 
assemble voƟng booths, arrange the room for an orderly ﬂow of traﬃc, situate the Help Desk clear of the Check-
In StaƟon, set up Sign Kiosk at the Help Desk, posiƟon the tabulator near the exit and at a distance from the 
voƟng booths. See Pre-ElecƟon Setup (p. 12). 
D. Technology 
Ensure Inspector Techs set up and power on the MiFi hotspot, electronic poll book(s), ballot-marking device(s) and 
the tabulator(s). For detailed procedures, see Precinct Manual for Poll Oﬃcials, VoƟng Systems OperaƟon (Ch. 7). 
MiFi Hotspot 
Provide the Inspector Techs with the Black Pouch from the Yellow Bag. They will complete opening procedures. 
Ballot-Marking Device 
Provide the Inspector Techs with the round barrel key from the Yellow Bag. They will complete opening 
procedures. 
 
Electronic Poll Book(s) 
Provide the Inspector Techs with the EViD acƟvator from the Black Pouch. They will complete opening 
procedures at the Check-In StaƟon. The Manager will set up the EViD Edge and the ExpressVote printer at the 
Help Desk. See Seƫng Up the EViD Edge Tablet (p. 60).  
 
Tabulator(s) 
Retrieve the Broken Seals Envelope from the Purple Bag and provide it along with the Black Pouch, the ﬂat silver 
key, and the ﬂat black key to two Inspector Techs to open polls on the tabulator(s). 
Once the technology is all operaƟonal and polls on the tabulator are opened, place the signed Zero Tape into the 
DS300 Tape Envelope in the Yellow Bag, return the Broken Seals Envelope to the Purple Bag, return the Black 
Pouch and all keys to the Yellow Bag. You may also choose to wear the keys on your wrist, if preferred. 
  
 
 
17 
E. Chain of Custody Log

Form Folder. 
Two relaƟves should not sign the Chain of Custody Log or any forms that require two ElecƟon Board 
Members to sign. When necessary, a non-relaƟve ElecƟon Board Member should parƟcipate and sign 
the form.

Chapter 4 – At the Help Desk 
(SecƟons 101.045, 101.5608, Fla. Stat.) 
The Help Desk is used to answer voter quesƟons, process all provisional ballots, and resolve voƟng situaƟons. 
Either the Manager or Assistant Manager should always occupy the Help Desk. If there is no one waiƟng for 
assistance at the Help Desk, the Manager or Assistant Manager should check in voters. If an EViD is available at 
the Check-In StaƟon and voters are waiƟng, the Assistant Manager should be posiƟoned at the Check-In StaƟon 
to check in voters. 
A.  Help Desk Tickets 
Voters will have a Help Desk Ticket in hand when they arrive at the Help Desk. The Ɵckets state, “Refer to Clerk” 
and indicate the nature of the assistance required (provisional required, address change, etc.).  
If a voter arrives at the Help Desk with an AcƟon Required Ticket, then they are at the incorrect precinct. See 
Wrong Precinct (p. 50). 
If there is a line at the Help Desk, taking a quick glance at the Ɵckets of the next few voters in line may allow you 
to provide voters with a MulƟpurpose Form or Provisional Ballot Envelope to ﬁll out while they wait.  
Place discarded Help Desk Tickets/AcƟon Required Tickets in the Completed Documents Envelope. Call the 
Manager Hotline for assistance with any Help Desk situaƟons not covered in this Handbook.  
  
Help Desk Tickets AcƟon Required Ticket 
 
 
25 
B. Multipurpose Form 
(SecƟons 101.045, 101.048, 101.031, 101.051, 101.49 Fla. Stat.) 
The MulƟpurpose Form combines four common forms into one convenient form: 
 Florida Voter RegistraƟon ApplicaƟon 
 DeclaraƟon to Secure Assistance  
 DeclaraƟon to Provide Assistance 
 Signature Aﬃdavit 
Only one form is needed per voter with the relevant secƟons completed. A voter must only complete the secƟon 
of the form that applies to the voter’s situaƟon. Completed MulƟpurpose Forms must be ﬁled in the Completed 
MulƟpurpose Form Envelope and returned in the Yellow Bag.  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
  
SECTION 1 Florida Voter RegistraƟon ApplicaƟon 
 
26 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
MulƟpurpose Forms do not have our contact informaƟon and are intended for use within the polling room.  
If a voter does not have the last 4 digits of their Social Security Number or their FL Driver License or FL 
IdenƟﬁcaƟon Card Number, they may update their registraƟon by compleƟng a Change of Name or 
Change of Legal Residence Form instead. This form is found in the Brown Form Folder.

C. Name Change 
(SecƟon 101.045(2), Fla. Stat.)  
Name changes are only to be made at the Help Desk. Name changes may only 
be made aŌer the voter completes SecƟon 1 of the MulƟpurpose Form for a 
“Record Update/Change.” If you have a voter’s ID number, write it in the 
“Oﬃcial use only” box on the line beside “FVRS No.” Do not write anywhere 
else in the box or on the applicaƟon. See MulƟpurpose Form (p. 25). 
 
To complete a name change on the EViD Edge: 
1. Search for the voter. When the screen asks if the voter’s informaƟon is correct, select “No.”  
 
 
2. When prompted to conﬁrm that you want to make a name/address change, select “Yes.”  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 

 
 
29 
 
3. Select the name you want to change and it will turn blue. Begin typing over the old name (you can also 
backspace to clear the name ﬁeld). Once you have entered the correct name, select “ConƟnue.”  
 
 
4. Verify that you have entered the correct name as wriƩen on the MulƟpurpose Form. When prompted to 
conﬁrm that the name and address are correct, select “Yes.”  
5. ConƟnue to process the voter as normal.  
If the voter’s former name is not on the precinct register, you cannot complete the name change request at the 
precinct. Instead, instruct the voter to complete SecƟon 1 of the MulƟpurpose Form for a “New RegistraƟon” 
and then issue a provisional ballot. See Voter Not Found (p. 48). File the completed MulƟpurpose Form in the 
Completed MulƟpurpose Form Envelope.  
  

 
30 
D. Address Change  
(SecƟon 101.045(2), Fla. Stat.) 
 
If the voter’s name appears on the precinct register but the voter indicates a change or diﬀerent address than 
the address listed, you will need to make a change of address. The procedures vary by the type of address 
change required. Address changes may only be made on the EViD if the voter is moving from one Duval County 
address to another Duval County address. Address changes may only be made at the Help Desk. 
Registered in Duval County 
Instruct the voter to complete SecƟon 1 of the MulƟpurpose Form for a “Record Update/Change.”  
To complete an address change on the EViD Edge:  
1. Search for the voter. When the screen asks if the voter’s informaƟon is correct, select “No.”  
 
 
2. When prompted to conﬁrm that you want to make a name/address change, select “Yes.” 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 

 
 
31 
3. Select “Clear Address.” When prompted to conﬁrm that you want to clear address ﬁeld, select “Yes.” 
 
4. Select address ﬁeld and begin to type the new address unƟl you see the address you are looking for in 
the drop-down menu. Select the correct address from the drop-down menu and then select “ConƟnue.” 
If the address does not appear in the drop-down opƟons, check for typos. Otherwise, select “ConƟnue.”  
 
5. Verify that you have selected the correct address. When prompted to conﬁrm that the name and 
address are correct, select “Yes.”  
6. ConƟnue to process the voter as normal. 
AŌer a voter updates their address at the polls, they may learn that they are no longer at the correct polling 
place. Select “Yes” when asked if the voter wants to vote at their correct polling place. In this case, an AcƟon 
Required Ticket will print from the EViD showing their correct polling place. Give the AcƟon Required Ticket to 
the voter, along with their ID, and say: “You are not in your newly assigned polling place. This Ɵcket shows the 
name and address of your correct locaƟon. You may bring this Ɵcket with you for your convenience.”  

 
32 
If the voter is not in the proper precinct but insists on voƟng there, select “No.” See Wrong Precinct (p. 50) to issue 
a provisional ballot. File the MulƟpurpose Form in the Completed MulƟpurpose Form Envelope.   
Residence Address Cannot Be Located 
If you see the following screen while compleƟng 
an in-county address change, you MUST call the 
Technology Hotline so that we can conﬁrm that 
the address exists in that precinct. Do not proceed without 
further instrucƟons. 
Registered in Another County 
Instruct the voter to complete SecƟon 1 of the MulƟpurpose 
Form for a “Record Update/Change”. Once the voter has 
completed the form, contact the Manager Hotline to verify 
the informaƟon from the form. The Manager Hotline will import the voter’s registraƟon informaƟon into the 
Duval County system to reﬂect the address change. Within a few minutes of the veriﬁcaƟon call, you should be 
able to either check in the voter or print an AcƟon Required Ticket and provide that to the voter to direct them 
to the correct precinct. If the address is not updated in the EViD within 15 minutes of your ﬁrst aƩempt on the 
Manager Hotline, the voter can conƟnue to wait, or vote a provisional ballot. It is the voter’s choice. You must 
advise the voter that the signature on the provisional ballot envelope must match the signature on ﬁle for the 
ballot to be counted. 
If the voter is not in the proper precinct but insists on voƟng there, see Wrong Precinct (p. 50) to issue a 
provisional ballot. File the MulƟpurpose Form in the Completed MulƟpurpose Form Envelope. 
Not Registered in Florida 
Instruct the voter to complete SecƟon 1 of the MulƟpurpose Form for a “New RegistraƟon”. Because the voter 
did not register to vote in Florida before book closing, the only opƟon for the voter is to vote a provisional ballot. 
See Voter Not Found (p. 48). 

 
 
33 
Address Protected 
If a voter’s address is marked on the precinct register as “Address Protected,” contact the Manager Hotline to 
allow the voter to disclose their current address and, if necessary, make an address change in a conﬁdenƟal and 
discreet manner. Protected addresses cannot be changed on the EViD. Make sure the voter completes SecƟon 1 
of the MulƟpurpose Form (Voter RegistraƟon) with their updated address. 
 
Name/Address Change Without Veriﬁers 
If a voter who wishes to update their registraƟon does not have their Florida driver license, Florida ID card or the 
last four numbers of their Social Security number, they cannot use the MulƟpurpose Form for a name/address 
change. Instead, they must use the Change of Name or Change of Legal Residence Form (p. 27). 
 
E. Spoiling a Ballot  
(SecƟon 101.5608(2), Fla. Stat.)  
A voter who makes an error on their ballot may obtain another ballot upon returning the ballot in its secrecy 
sleeve to the Help Desk. The voter may be given no more than three ballots.  
1. Instruct the voter to remove the ballot from the secrecy sleeve and fold the ballot in half to preserve as 
much privacy as possible. Instruct the voter to slightly tear the ballot through the Ɵming marks, but to 
not tear the ballot completely in half. Place the altered ballot in the Spoiled Ballot Envelope. The voter or 
a poll worker can spoil the ballot. Allow the voter to choose. If the voter leaves without altering the 
ballot, a poll worker should physically alter the ballot and place it in the Spoiled Ballot Envelope. 
2. Escort the voter to the Ballot StaƟon to obtain a new ballot.  
 Ask the Inspector Tech at the Ballot StaƟon for the voter’s VoƟng Pass from the EViD Accordion 
Folder. Do not undo the voter’s check-in. 
 Issue another ballot. See Precinct Manual for Poll Oﬃcials, Voter Asks for Another Ballot (Ch. 5).  
3. Politely ask the voter if they would like assistance in voƟng or oﬀer the use of the ballot-marking device. 
4. If the voter makes a mistake on their third ballot, instruct the voter to write on the ballot to make their 
intent clear. Explain to the voter that they must insert the unscanned third ballot into the Gray Bag.  
Do not give a voter a fourth ballot under any circumstance! 
 
If a voter, who aŌer checking-in, is given a ballot and then returns the ballot to a poll worker before casƟng it 
and indicates that they do not desire to vote, the ballot, even if blank, shall be treated as a spoiled ballot and 
shall not be tabulated. Place it in the Spoiled Ballot Envelope. To prevent the voter from having a voter history, 
the poll worker should undo the voter check-in on the EViD.  
 
If the check-in of the voter who abandoned the ballot cannot be undone, then place the unscanned ballot in the 
Gray Bag. When the polls close, the Manager will perform ballot accounƟng procedures to account for all ballots 
issued and the number of ballots spoiled. See Ballot ReconciliaƟon (p. 81). 
 
 

 
34 
F. Voter Is Challenged at the Polling Place 
(SecƟon 101.111, Fla. Stat.) 
If a challenge is presented, direct the person challenging the voter and the 
voter not to interact with one another. 
 
1. Instruct the person entering the challenge that they must complete an 
Oath of Person Entering Challenge form (in Brown Form Folder).   
2. Inform the person challenging the voter that any elector or Poll 
Watcher ﬁling a frivolous challenge of any person’s right to vote 
commits a misdemeanor of the ﬁrst degree. 
3. The Manager must witness the oath. AŌer the person challenging the 
voter executes the oath, the challenged voter must be immediately 
presented with the carbon copy (yellow) of the challenge. 
4. The challenged voter must vote a provisional ballot.  
5. The Manager must staple the original Oath of Person Entering 
Challenge (white) securely to the outside of the Provisional Ballot 
Envelope and place it in the Orange Bag. 
 
For instrucƟons on how to process a challenged voter on the EViD Edge, see Provisional Ballots, Challenged Voter 
(p.53). 
ExcepƟon: If the sole basis of a challenge is that the voter’s legal residence is not in the precinct, the challenged 
voter shall be ﬁrst given the opportunity to do a change of address. Follow the procedures in Address Change (p. 
30) before conƟnuing the voter check-in process.  
G. Voter’s Signatures Differ  
(SecƟon 101.49 Fla. Stat.) 
If a Manager, upon a just comparison of the signatures, doubts that the signature on the idenƟﬁcaƟon presented 
by the elector is the same as the signature the elector provided at check-in, the Manager can have the voter 
complete BOTH SecƟon 4 of the MulƟpurpose Form (Signature Aﬃdavit) to acknowledge the diﬀerence in 
signature and then complete SecƟon 1 of the MulƟpurpose Form (Voter RegistraƟon ApplicaƟon) to update the 
voter’s signature on record. Place the completed MulƟpurpose Form in the Completed MulƟpurpose Form 
Envelope. Check in the voter and issue a VoƟng Pass.  
If the voter refuses to sign the Signature Aﬃdavit but insists on voƟng, he or she must vote a provisional ballot 
following the procedure in Provisional Ballots (p. 58).  
 
Having a voter cast a provisional ballot due to refusal to sign the Signature Aﬃdavit should be a last 
resort and a very rare occurrence since you can largely verify the voter’s idenƟty from their photo on 
the valid ID.  
 
 
 
 
Oath of Person Entering Challenge 
 
 
35 
H. Voter Needs Assistance  
(SecƟons 101.051, 101.075, 101.031(4), 97.061 Fla. Stat.) 
Assistance is not required but must be provided if requested by the voter. In some cases, a voter will bring someone 
to assist them. Voters who may be sent to the Help Desk with their VoƟng Pass for assistance fall into two groups: 
Voter Coded “Eligible for Assistance” 
If the voter’s VoƟng Pass states “Eligible for Assistance:” 
1. Instruct the person assisƟng the voter to complete SecƟon 3, DeclaraƟon to Provide Assistance, on the 
MulƟpurpose Form. 
2. Accept the completed form and then direct the voter and their assistant to the Ballot StaƟon to obtain 
their ballot. 
3. Place the completed form in the Completed MulƟpurpose Form Envelope. 
Voter Not Coded “Eligible for Assistance” 
If the voter’s VoƟng Pass does not state “Eligible for Assistance,” but the voter requests assistance: 
1. Instruct the voter to complete SecƟon 2, DeclaraƟon to Secure Assistance on the MulƟpurpose Form. 
The Manager may help the voter complete the form if they request it.  
2. If the voter wishes to have their registraƟon updated to indicate “Eligible for Assistance” for future 
elecƟons, instruct them to also complete SecƟon 1 and check the boxes for “Record Update/Change” 
and “I will need help voƟng.” 
3. The person assisƟng the voter completes SecƟon 3, DeclaraƟon to Provide Assistance. If two poll workers 
assist the voter, they do not need to complete SecƟon 3. 
4. Accept the completed form and then direct the voter and their assistant to the Ballot StaƟon to obtain 
their ballot. 
5. Place the completed form in the Completed MulƟpurpose Form Envelope. 
 
If poll workers provide assistance, two poll workers from diﬀerent party aﬃliaƟons must assist the 
voter, if present, or, if not, two poll workers of same poliƟcal party, shall assist the voter. A voter’s 
employer, agent of an employer, or an oﬃcer or agent of the voter’s union may not assist a voter. 
 
Power of AƩorney is not valid regarding voƟng. A voter must sign (or mark) any precinct register, form 
or aﬃdavit for themselves. 
 
Voting Accessibility 
As a Manager, you should be familiar with voter accessibility resources available at the Help Desk. Provide these 
to the voter as needed. 
The voter accessibility kit available at the Help Desk includes the following items from the Blue Bag:  
 Ball cap with head pointer – Pointer aƩaches to the ball cap to be used to touch the ExpressVote screen 
 Surgical head caps – Placed on the head under the ball cap with head pointer  
 Headphone guards – Placed on the headphones before use  
 Signature guides – Can be used to assist someone in signing a document within space provided 
 Magniﬁer sheet – Can be used to enlarge text on ballots or forms 

 
36 
The voter accessibility kit available at the Help Desk also includes the following items from the Brown Form Folder:  
 Voter RegistraƟon InstrucƟons in Braille – Available in English/Spanish 
The ExpressVote ballot-marking device oﬀers addiƟonal accessibility features: 
 Modiﬁed screen appearance 
 Audio tacƟle keypad 
 AssisƟve technology connecƟons (port to connect a personal rocker switch device/sip and puﬀ device) 
 Headphones 
For more informaƟon on ExpressVote features, see “Ballot-Marking Device: Accessibility Features” , Precinct 
Manual for Poll Oﬃcials (Ch. 7). 
To assist a voter requesƟng use of ExpressVote, see Voter Requests Use of Ballot-Marking Device (p. 37). 
I. Voter Requests Language Assistance 
(U.S.C. 10303(f)(4) and 52 U.S.C. 10503(b)(2)(A))  
Some voters may need language assistance due to an inability to read, write, or speak English or because of 
limited English language proﬁciency. A voter needing assistance must ﬁll out the “DeclaraƟon to Secure 
Assistance” form if they are not marked “eligible for assistance” in the precinct register. A person assisƟng a 
voter must ﬁll out the “DeclaraƟon to Provide Assistance” form. 
Each polling place provides opportuniƟes for language assistance 
including: 
 All signs, noƟces, forms and ballots are available in Spanish. 
 A link to the ElecƟon Assistance Commission’s online 
bilingual elecƟon-related language assistance informaƟon is 
available in the Voter InformaƟon Binder. 
 The ExpressVote allows for wriƩen an audio language 
selecƟon in Spanish. 
 Bilingual poll workers wear idenƟﬁcaƟon indicaƟng diﬀerent 
languages spoken. 
 LangServ professional translator services for over 100 
languages are available by phone. See the Voter InformaƟon 
Binder for the LangServ page. 
For more, see “Voter Requests Language Assistance”, Precinct 
Manual for Poll Oﬃcials (Ch. 6). 
LangServ 
If a voter requests LangServ professional translator services: 
1. The voter will complete SecƟon 2 of the MulƟpurpose Form (DeclaraƟon to Secure Assistance). If 
requested by the voter, the Manager may assist the voter in compleƟng the form. 
2. Direct the voter to the LangServ page in the Voter InformaƟon Binder in the “Language Resources” 
secƟon. Ask the voter to please ﬁnd their language and point to it on the page.  
3. On the poll worker’s phone, dial the 1-904-552-9309 and enter the 2-digit code for the language 
requested. 
LangServ page 
 
 
37 
4. The interpreter will ask for the poll worker’s ﬁrst and last name and precinct number.  
5. Advise the voter that the interpreter is on the line. Put the phone on speaker so the voter and 
interpreter may begin their conversaƟon.  
 
 
 
 
J. Voter Requests Use of Ballot-Marking Device (ExpressVote) 
Any voter may request to use the ExpressVote ballot-marking device for any reason. The ExpressVote printer is 
aƩached to the EViD Edge at the Help Desk. A voter who chooses to vote using the ExpressVote will need to ﬁrst 
be checked in on the EViD Edge.  
 
LangServ instrucƟons 
 
38 
To acƟvate a ballot card:  
1. Check in the voter using the EViD Edge. Select the checkbox in the top leŌ corner to “Print ExpressVote 
AcƟvaƟon Card.”  
 
2. Verify that the name and address for the voter is correct and select “Yes.” 
3. The voter must select “Yes” to verify their informaƟon, sign the EViD screen, and select “OK.” 
4. Compare the voter’s signature to the ID presented. Select “Accept.” 
5. When prompted, insert a blank ballot card into the ExpressVote printer with the corner cut edge on the 
top right. Once inserted, the printer will automaƟcally take up the card and hold it unƟl it is ready to 
print.  
6. Select “OK” to print the ballot card. The voter’s acƟvated ballot card will be printed with a ballot style 
idenƟﬁer that the ExpressVote will recognize. Conﬁrm that the ballot style printed on the ballot card 
matches the ballot style shown on the VoƟng Pass.  
 
7. Return the voter’s ID and give them the ballot card and a white secrecy sleeve. 
8. The voter may proceed to the ExpressVote to mark the ballot. See Precinct Manual for Poll Oﬃcials, 
Ballot-Marking Device (Ch. 7) for voter instrucƟons. 
 
If a voter who requests to use the ballot-marking device is mistakenly checked in at the Check-In StaƟon instead 
of the Help Desk, you will need to reprint the ballot. See Reprint Ballot for instrucƟons (p. 74).  
 
K. Voter Goes to the Wrong Precinct 
If a voter aƩempts to check in and vote at the wrong precinct, the Inspector Tech at the Check-In StaƟon will 
print an AcƟon Required Ticket for the voter with the name and address of the correct polling place. Some 
Ballot style on ballot card 
 
 
39 
voters may request addiƟonal informaƟon at the Help Desk. Advise the voter that they may call (904)255-VOTE 
(8683) to learn more. If asked, a Manager should search for direcƟons to the correct polling place on their phone 
and write down the direcƟons for the voter. If a voter insists on voƟng at the wrong precinct, see Wrong Precinct 
(p. 50) to issue a provisional ballot. 
 
L. Voter Casts an Incorrect Ballot 
If a voter claims that he or she did not receive the correct ballot (i.e., wrong ballot style issued) BUT has 
already cast the ballot, the voter’s ballot may not be retrieved. The voter does not get another ballot. 
Issuing the wrong ballot to a voter may result in dismissal! Note the incident on the Trouble Log. 
M. Voter Leaves Without Casting Ballot 
(Rule 1S-2.034, Florida AdministraƟve Code) 
If a voter leaves the polling room and leaves a ballot in the voƟng booth or in and around the tabulator without 
casƟng the ballot, poll workers shall place the unscanned ballot in the Gray Bag to be provided to the Canvassing 
Board. Note the incident on the Trouble Log. See Trouble Log (p. 40). 
N. Voter Overvotes Contests 
If the voter mistakenly over votes the ballot by making more than one selecƟon in a contest, the tabulator will 
reject the ballot. The voter may come to the Help Desk to have the rejected ballot spoiled. See Spoiling a Ballot 
(p. 33). AŌer spoiling the ballot, escort the voter to the Ballot StaƟon to obtain a new ballot and secrecy sleeve, 
with instrucƟons on how to properly mark the ballot.  
If the voter insists on voƟng the rejected ballot “as-is,” inform the voter that the only way to cast a ballot that the 
tabulator rejects is to place the unscanned ballot in the Gray Bag. Allow the voter to cast the ballot into the Gray 
Bag. Note the incident on the Trouble Log. See Trouble Log (p. 40). 
O. Voter Tries to Take Ballot from Polling Room  
(SecƟon 104.20, Fla. Stat.) 
If a voter aƩempts to take their ballot from the polling place, advise them that 
it is against the law and that they will be reported. Explain to the voter that the 
ballot must be spoiled if the voter does not wish to have it counted. Make a 
notaƟon on the Final Ballot Report Form and document that a voter leŌ with a 
ballot on the Trouble Log if the voter insists on leaving the precinct with the 
ballot. See Trouble Log (p. 40). If a voter leaves before casƟng a ballot and their 
idenƟty is known, see “Undo Voter Check-In”, Precinct Manual for Poll Oﬃcials 
(Ch. 4). If the voter can be idenƟﬁed, an ElecƟons Fraud Complaint (Form 
DS_DE 34) must be completed. The form is located in the Voter InformaƟon 
Binder. Voter Fraud Hotline: 1-877-868-3737 
P. Voter is Deceased 
(SecƟon 98.075, Fla. Stat.) 
The Duval County Supervisor of ElecƟons shall remove the name of a deceased registered voter from the 
statewide voter registraƟon system upon receipt of a copy of a death cerƟﬁcate issued by a governmental 
agency authorized to issue death cerƟﬁcates. A voter may inform you of a person who is deceased but sƟll 
ElecƟons Fraud Complaint Form 
 
40 
registered to vote. To have the deceased voter removed from the voter registraƟon system, the interested 
person may mail, fax, email, or hand-deliver a photocopy of the death cerƟﬁcate for the deceased voter to the 
Duval County Supervisor of ElecƟons Oﬃce. For addiƟonal informaƟon, advise the interested person to call the 
Duval County Supervisor of ElecƟons Oﬃce at (904) 255-VOTE (8683). Once the Supervisor of ElecƟons has 
received the death cerƟﬁcate, the voter’s name may be removed from the voter registraƟon system. 
Q. Trouble Log 
The Trouble Log is used by the Manager or Assistant Manager primarily to document problems with the 
electronic pollbooks, tabulator and the ballot-marking device. However, it may also be used to record other 
signiﬁcant or unusual problems which occur on ElecƟon Day. Be sure to record the Ɵmes that the problems 
occurred and were resolved.   
Examples would be:  
 Problems with the MiFi hotspot 
 Incorrect or insuﬃcient ballots received from the ElecƟons 
Center 
 Missing EViD acƟvators or memory sƟcks 
 People taking pictures in the polling place (other than their 
own ballot) 
 Disorder in the polling place 
 AƩempts by unauthorized individuals to gain access to the 
polling place 
 Repairs needed to ElecƟons Oﬃce equipment 
 Accidents, injuries and illness 
 Damage to polling place facility or property 
 Voter leaves with ballot 
 Ballot card reprinted 
 
R. Injury, Incident, or Illness Reports  
Your safety and well-being are our top priority. As a poll worker, you may encounter situaƟons where you or 
someone else gets sick, injured, or involved in an incident at the polling place. These situaƟons can be stressful 
and challenging, but it is important to handle them calmly and professionally. See Safety Brieﬁng (p. 15).  
For all incidents the following procedures apply: 
1. Assess the situaƟon and provide care. This may include providing ﬁrst aid or calling 911. For facility or 
property damage that interrupts the ﬂow of voƟng through the polling place, this may include alerƟng 
voters by marking the damage with one of the small orange cones or posiƟoning a poll worker to 
redirect voters away from the incident. VoƟng should conƟnue without interrupƟon. 
2. Call the Manager Hotline immediately. Report the incident to the Duval County Supervisor of ElecƟons 
staﬀ and follow any provided instrucƟons.  
3. Provide wriƩen documentaƟon of the incident. You must document any work-related injuries, illnesses, 
accidents, trip-and-falls, or any other incidents.  
□ Complete the relevant risk management form (see below). 
Trouble Log 

 
 
41 
□ Write a note on the top of the form if the individual will seek medical aƩenƟon or ﬁle a claim. 
Indicate this by wriƟng “Medical: (Yes, No or Unsure)” and “Claim: (Yes, No or Unsure).” 
□ Take photos of the locaƟon of the incident as well as the injury to person or damage to property. 
Email photos to duvalpollingplaces@coj.net within 24 hours and indicate in the subject line the 
precinct name or number. 
□ Note the incident on the Trouble Log.  
 
Risk Management Forms 
 
Voter/Citizen is Affected 
The voter or ciƟzen aﬀected will complete the CiƟzen 
Injury Incident Report Form located in the Forms 
Folder. The form should be signed by the Manager. File 
in the Injury Incident or Illness Report Form Envelope.  
Poll Worker is Affected 
The poll worker aﬀected or the Manager will complete 
the First Report of Injury Form located in the Forms 
Folder. The form should be signed by the Manager. File 
in the Injury Incident or Illness Report Form Envelope.  
Damage  
Note any incident which causes damage to the polling 
place facility or property on the Trouble Log. See 
Trouble Log (p. 40). 
 
S. Rovers  
Rovers are technical support staﬀ that assist with technical issues at polling places. They are the eyes and ears of 
the ElecƟons Oﬃce out in the ﬁeld. They also have extra supplies for your precinct, if needed. 
 
Rovers are not part of your staﬀ. When a Rover arrives at your polling place, the Manager must check their ID 
badge before allowing them into the polling room. If you are not expecƟng a Rover at your polling place, call the 
Manager Hotline to verify.  
 
If you need the assistance of a Rover at any Ɵme on ElecƟon Day, contact the Technology Hotline ﬁrst. The 
contact informaƟon for your assigned Rover can be found on the Blue Polling Place Sheet and can be used as a 
backup. 
 
T. Poll Watchers 
(SecƟons 101.111, 101.131, 101.23, 102.031(3), Fla. Stat.) 
Each party, each poliƟcal commiƩee, and each candidate may choose to appoint Poll Watchers to observe polling 
place procedures on ElecƟon Day. Only one Poll Watcher per party, poliƟcal commiƩee, or candidate is allowed 
in the polling place at any Ɵme during voƟng.  
Risk management forms 
 
42 
 
The Duval County Supervisor of ElecƟons approves Poll Watcher 
designaƟons and reserves the right to reject any Poll Watcher. The 
Supervisor of ElecƟons will provide each polling place a list of the 
names of pre-approved Poll Watchers. 
 
Upon acceptance of Poll Watcher designaƟons, the Supervisor of 
ElecƟons will issue ID badges to the candidate, party, or commiƩee, 
who will then distribute them to the individual Poll Watchers.  
 
The Manager will designate an area near Check-In StaƟon for Poll 
Watchers. Poll Watchers must follow all guidelines provided in 
Precinct Manual for Poll Oﬃcials, In the Polling Room (Ch. 3). A 
Manager must call the Manager Hotline to report any potenƟal 
violaƟon of these guidelines. The Manager should provide the name 
and ID badge number of the Poll Watcher. All Poll Watchers may be 
given one warning by the Duval County Supervisor of ElecƟons 
Oﬃce. If there is another potenƟal violaƟon, call the Manager 
Hotline again. 
 
 
U. Exit Pollsters 
(SecƟon 102.031(4)(b), Fla. Stat.) 
An elecƟon exit poll is a poll of voters taken immediately aŌer they have exited the polling places. Exit Pollsters 
conduct exit polls to gain an early indicaƟon as to how an elecƟon has turned out. 
Exit Pollsters: 
 Cannot wear clothing that references candidates, parƟes or causes. 
 Must conduct polling acƟviƟes out of earshot of voters. 
 Must abide by the Manager’s indicaƟon of where they may stand to conduct polling. 
 
Exit Pollsters are allowed in the No SolicitaƟon Zone if they are only speaking to voters exiƟng the polling 
room. 
  
List of pre-approved poll watchers

Chapter 5 – Provisional Ballots  
(52 U.S.C. SecƟon 21082, SecƟons 101.048, 101.049, 101.111, 101.043, 101.045, Fla. Stat.)  
List of situaƟons in which a voter must be allowed to vote a provisional ballot:  
IdenƟﬁcaƟon  A voter who does not produce an acceptable form of photo ID with signature or a 
photo ID and a signature ID.  
 A voter whose Florida driver license number or Florida idenƟﬁcaƟon card number, 
or the last 4 digits of their social security number has not been veriﬁed.  
 A person whose signature on the precinct register diﬀers from that on the 
idenƟﬁcaƟon presented and the person refuses to complete a Signature Aﬃdavit.  
 A voter whose idenƟty is in quesƟon based on the photo idenƟﬁcaƟon provided.  
RegistraƟon  A person whose name is not on the precinct register and the poll worker is unable 
to verify whether or not the person is a registered voter of the state.  
 A person whose name is not on the precinct register and the poll worker veriﬁes 
that the person is not registered in the state, but the person maintains that he or 
she is enƟtled to vote.  
 A person whose name is not on the precinct register and who has completed 
either a Voter RegistraƟon ApplicaƟon or the Change of Name or Change of Legal 
Residence Form but the poll worker is unable to verify whether the person is a 
registered voter of the state.  
 Any person for whom you are unable to get through to the Supervisor of ElecƟons’ 
oﬃce to determine if person is eligible to vote.  
Address Change  A registered voter needs to make an out-of-county address change in the polling 
place, and the Manager Hotline cannot be reached. 
 A registered voter who is required but refuses to execute an address change on an 
aﬃrmaƟon or Voter RegistraƟon ApplicaƟon in order to vote a regular ballot.  
VoƟng Status   A voter whose name is on the precinct register with an indicaƟon that they have 
already voted but the voter maintains they have not already voted in this elecƟon.  
 A voter whose name is on the precinct register with an indicaƟon that they 
requested a vote-by-mail ballot and the poll worker conﬁrms that the Supervisor 
of ElecƟons has received the voted vote-by-mail ballot, but the voter maintains 
that they have not returned the vote-by-mail ballot (only applies to Paper 
Register). 
 A person who an elecƟon oﬃcial has asserted is not eligible and a notaƟon is 
indicated on the precinct register. 
Voter Challenge  A voter who has been challenged except in speciﬁc cases of address changes. 
Poll Closed  A voter who votes on or aŌer the normal poll closing Ɵme pursuant to a court or 
other order extending the polling hours.  
 
Each person voƟng a provisional ballot must be given the “NoƟce of Rights” and instrucƟons required by law 
(located on the provisional envelope tear oﬀ ﬂap):  
 Right to present further wriƩen evidence (if he or she so chooses) that supports their eligibility to vote 
to the Duval County Supervisor of Elections by no later than 5 p.m. on the second day following the election.  
 Right to find out after the election whether the provisional ballot was counted and if not, the reason why. 
 Right to cure signature if signature is missing from provisional ballot cerƟﬁcate or the signature on the 
cerƟﬁcate does not match with the one on record or in the precinct register.  
 
Never deny a voter the opportunity to cast a provisional ballot. The Canvassing Board will determine 
the voter’s eligibility and whether to count the provisional ballot. 

 
44 
A. Prepare the Provisional Ballot Envelope 
Give the voter the Provisional Ballot Envelope to complete SecƟon 1 in full. These are available in English 
and Spanish. The voter must complete and sign the form in front of the poll worker. If it is not signed, it will 
be rejected. The Manager and Assistant Manager who witness the voter’s signature must complete and sign 
SecƟon 2 in full and indicate the elecƟon date, precinct and ballot style provided. The Manager or Assistant 
Manager must also complete SecƟon 3 by checking the box beside the Reason for Provisional Ballot. Do not 
mark anything else. If a voter needs to complete a MulƟpurpose Form because their name/address has 
changed, do not put the completed MulƟpurpose Form in the Provisional Ballot Envelope. MulƟpurpose 
Forms always go in the Completed MulƟpurpose Form Envelope.

No ID Provided 
A voter may present you with a Help Desk Ticket indicaƟng “VOTER DID NOT PROVIDE PICTURE/SIGNATURE ID.” 
If a voter cannot provide a valid photo and signature ID, the voter is allowed to vote a provisional ballot. For a list 
of acceptable idenƟﬁcaƟon, see Precinct Manual for Poll Oﬃcials, Photo IdenƟﬁcaƟon (Ch. 4). 
 
AŌer the Provisional Ballot Envelope is completed:

Wrong Precinct

Challenged Before Polls Open 
A voter may present you with a Help Desk Ticket indicaƟng “CHALLENGED VOTER IS CLAIMING ELIGIBILITY .” This

challenged prior to the opening of the polls may vote a provisional ballot. A copy of the challenge form which 
was completed prior to the opening of the polls will be in the AdministraƟve Folder. Present the copy of the 
challenge form to the voter immediately. To issue a provisional ballot to this voter, follow the procedures for 
Other Provisional (p. 57). 
 
Challenged at the Precinct 
A voter who does not present a Help Desk Ticket, but whose eligibility is challenged by an individual at the 
polling place, may vote a provisional ballot. See instrucƟons at Voter Is Challenged at the Polling Place (p. 34) 
prior to issuing a provisional ballot to the voter.

A voter may present you with a Help Desk Ticket indicaƟng “PARTY NOT VOTING.” It is common for some voters

dispute their party aﬃliaƟon may vote a provisional ballot. Say to the voter, “Florida is a closed primary state. 
You may only vote in the primary for the party in which you are registered.” See Precinct Manual for Poll 
Oﬃcials, Types of ElecƟons (Ch. 1). If the voter requests to be registered in a diﬀerent party and vote, have the 
voter complete SecƟon 1 of the MulƟpurpose Form prior to compleƟng the Provisional Ballot Envelope. File the

If the OK buƩon is gray, it will not allow you to proceed because you have chosen an incorrect ballot

Pending Status 
A voter may present you with a Help Desk Ticket indicaƟng “VOTER STATUS IS PENDING.” In Florida, book closing 
is 29 days before each elecƟon. People who are not registered to vote by that date are not eligible to vote in the 
next elecƟon. Florida does not have same-day voter registraƟon. AŌer searching for and ﬁnding a voter, the EViD 
may show a message that the voter’s status is “Pending”. Say the following: “The system is telling me that you 
registered aŌer the deadline for this elecƟon. However, you may be able to vote a regular ballot in the next 
elecƟon.” The voter may vote a provisional ballot. See Other Provisional (p. 57). 
Other Provisional (Pending Status, Already Voted, Ineligible, Special Handling, etc.) 
Voters may vote a provisional ballot for any of the reasons indicated in Provisional Ballots (p. 43). In many of 
these situaƟons, you will be prompted to issue a provisional ballot automaƟcally instead of being allowed to 
print a VoƟng Pass.

 Verify that the name and address for the voter is correct and select “Yes.” 
 Select “Yes” when prompted to issue a provisional ballot. 
 Enter the provisional ballot tracking number (twice) and select “OK.” 
 The voter must select “Yes” to verify their informaƟon, sign the EViD screen, and select “OK.” 
 Compare the voter’s signature to the ID presented. Select “Accept” and the VoƟng Pass will print.

SOE.” If you see this message, call the Manager Hotline for special instrucƟons. Once the call is 
completed, select “Yes” or “No” as advised when prompted on the screen.  
Voter’s Signatures Differ/Voter Refuses to Sign Afﬁdavit 
 (SecƟons 98.461, 101.5608(1), 101.49 Fla. Stat.) 
A voter may present you with a Help Desk Ticket indicaƟng “VOTER SIGNATURE REJECTED.” If the voter whose 
signatures diﬀer refuses to sign the Signature Aﬃdavit but insists on voƟng, he or she must vote a provisional 
ballot. For more informaƟon, see Voter’s Signatures Diﬀer (p. 34). 
AŌer the Provisional Ballot Envelope is completed:  
 Review the valid photo ID. Scan the ID, if possible, or select “Voter Check-In Search” on the Home screen. 
 Enter the voter’s date of birth and iniƟals (ﬁrst and last) in the correct ﬁelds. Select “Search.” 
 Verify that the name and address for the voter is correct and select “Yes.” 
 The voter must select “Yes” to verify their informaƟon, sign the EViD screen, and select “OK.”

 Enter the provisional ballot tracking number (twice) and select “OK” and the VoƟng Pass will print. 
 
 
C. Issue a Ballot to the Provisional Ballot Voter 
 Before issuing a ballot or ballot card, ﬁll in completely the provisional 
review box in the upper right corner of the ballot where “Oﬃce Use 
Only” is indicated. Filling in this box will prevent the ballot from being 
mistakenly cast in the tabulator.  
 Select the ballot style printed on the VoƟng Pass.  
 Record the stub number on the VoƟng Pass.  
 Give the voter the paper ballot or ballot card and pink secrecy sleeve 
and direct the voter to the voƟng booth closest to the Help Desk and 
advise them to bring the ballot back to the Help Desk. Hold on to the 
Provisional Ballot Envelope unƟl they return. 
 While waiƟng for the voter to return, add the name of the voter, the 
reason the provisional ballot was issued, the ballot style number and 
the Manager or Assistant Manager’s iniƟals to the List of Provisional 
Voters stored in the Orange Bag. 
 Staple the VoƟng Pass to the Provisional Ballot Envelope. 
 
D. Seal the Provisional Ballot Envelope 
 Once the voter has marked their provisional ballot, the voter must 
place the voted ballot (which may be a voted printed ballot or ballot 
card that contains the voter’s selecƟons) into the pink secrecy sleeve, 
then place the pink secrecy sleeve into the Provisional Ballot 
Envelope. Nothing else should be in the envelope! 
 The voter should seal the Provisional Ballot Envelope. The NoƟce of 
Rights is located on the envelope ﬂap. Say to the voter, “You may 
tear oﬀ the ﬂap to track your ballot on our website at 
duvalelecƟons.gov.” 
 Place the voted Provisional Ballot Envelope in the Orange Bag. 
 
Absolutely no tabulaƟon of provisional ballots! Do not cast, or allow a voter to cast, a provisional 
ballot through the tabulaƟng equipment at the polls.

Chapter 6 – EViD Edge Tablet  
The EViD Edge is an electronic poll book used at the Help Desk.

B. Setting Up the EViD Edge 
1. Gently place the gray hard case on the Help Desk. Unlatch all four latches, and then remove the lid 
completely. 
2. If there is Styrofoam packing in the gray hard case around the EViD Edge, remove it. Do not remove the 
EViD Edge or the cables. Also, do not unplug the Edge printer. 
3. Next, remove the power cord and the power block from the back of the gray hard case, and connect 
them.

4. Plug the power cord into the matching port on the back of the EViD Edge if it is not already plugged in. 
Plug the other end of the power cord into a wall outlet or power strip. Check the indicator on the screen 
to conﬁrm that you are not on baƩery power. 
5. Turn on the Edge printer by pressing and holding the power buƩon. 
6. Next, remove the ExpressVote printer, power cord and power block, and the USB cord from the black 
laptop case. 
7. Plug one end of the power cord into the DC port on the back of the ExpressVote printer, and the other 
end into a wall outlet or power strip. 
8. Plug the USB cord into the USB port on the back of the ExpressVote printer, and the other end into the 
USB port on the EViD Edge. 
9. Press and hold the orange power buƩon on the ExpressVote printer for two seconds to turn it on. 
10. Insert the acƟvator into the acƟvator port on the right side of the EViD Edge. If the message “WaiƟng for 
AcƟvator” appears aŌer inserƟng the acƟvator, move it to the extra acƟvator port. 
11. Press the gray soŌ-touch buƩon on the right side of the EViD Edge. AŌer a moment, a green status light 
appears if powered up correctly. 
12. Verify that the polling place is correct and select “Yes.” Verify that the date and Ɵme is correct and select 
"Yes.” If either is not correct, call the Technology Hotline immediately. 
 

 
62 
13. Use the on-screen keyboard to enter your iniƟals, and then enter your EViD 
Clerk Code found on the Blue Polling Place Sheet. Select “Submit.” Your iniƟals 
will be on each VoƟng Pass that you issue. If you are going to step away from 
the EViD Edge, select the white human ﬁgure icon on the Home screen to 
remove your iniƟals.  
14. An Opening Report automaƟcally prints. Tear oﬀ the Opening Report, sign it 
where indicated, and place it in the front of the EViD Accordion Folder. 
15. Unplug the acƟvator from the USB port and return it to the Black Pouch. 
 
 
 
You must enter the EViD code on the EViD Edge every Ɵme the iniƟals are changed to keep it in Clerk 
Mode. 
 
C. Using the EViD Edge (Check-In and Undo Check-In) 
The EViD Edge performs the same funcƟons and has the same check-in screens as an EViD Compact at the 
Check-In StaƟon. The EViD Edge also allows Managers to issue provisional ballots and complete name/address 
changes. Voter signatures are captured on the EViD Edge screen instead of a signature pad. For full check-in 
procedures, see Precinct Manual for Poll Oﬃcials, VoƟng Process (Ch. 4).   
ID Scan on the EViD Edge 
If a voter provides a valid Florida driver license or Florida ID 
card, you should scan it using the EViD Edge scanner aƩached 
to the right-side of the EViD Edge. Set the ID on the scanner 
with the barcode facing up, then press the yellow buƩon on 
the side of the EViD to use the scanner. The voter’s 
registraƟon informaƟon will appear on the screen. If the 
scanner is not aligned properly to scan the barcode, the tray 
can be removed and reposiƟoned. 
Voter Signature on the EViD Edge 
(SecƟons 98.461, 101.49, 101.5608 Fla. Stat.) 
The EViD Edge has the same check-in procedures as the EViD, except for capturing voter signatures.  
***** 

 
 
63 
When you have found the voter on the EViD Edge, you will be prompted to verify the voter’s name on the 
screen. When asked if the informaƟon matches, select “Yes.”  
The next screen will appear upside down to the poll worker. The tablet should be Ɵlted toward the voter so that 
they may verify their name, date of birth, and address. Say to the voter, “Please verify that all your informaƟon 
shown is correct. If everything is correct, please press ‘Yes.’” If the voter selects “No” when asked if their name, 
address, and date of birth are correct, see Address Change (p. 30). 
 
Once the voter selects “Yes,” the next screen will appear. Say to the voter, “Please sign on the line using the 
stylus or your ﬁnger and select “OK” when you are done.” If the voter hits the “Clear” buƩon, they will have to 
re-sign.  
When the voter selects “OK” the EViD screen will change orientaƟon so that the informaƟon is facing the poll 
worker. Tilt the tablet back toward the poll worker.  
Compare the voter’s signature on the EViD with the signature on the valid ID. If the signatures appear to be the 
same, select “Accept.”  
 
The rest of the procedure is the same as found in the Precinct Manual for Poll Oﬃcials, VoƟng Process (Ch. 4).

Changing the EViD Edge Printer Tape 
The Edge printer is used to print VoƟng Passes and AcƟon Required Tickets at the Help Desk. Extra rolls of EViD 
printer paper are in the Box of EViD Paper at the Check-In StaƟon. To remove the printer roll, press the orange

is on the correct side for prinƟng. Snap the lid closed. If you see a red error light, that means the lid was not
