Chapter 7 –Troubleshooting 
A. DS300 Malfunction 
The tabulator should remain locked during voƟng hours except in the event of a malfuncƟon. If the problems 
with the tabulator cannot be solved using the informaƟon below, contact the Technology Hotline immediately.  
Device Not Ready for Voting/Election Deﬁnition Not Found 
If you see a status on the tabulator indicaƟng “VoƟng Device Not Ready” or “ElecƟon DeﬁniƟon not found,” the 
memory sƟck may not be plugged in securely.  
 
Use the ﬂat black key to open the front access compartment. Remove memory sƟck, reinsert and press down to 
securely seat it into the port.  
Power Status 
On the top right corner of the 
display, if a baƩery icon is showing 
instead of an AC power plug icon, 
then the tabulator may not be 
plugged into the AC power cord. It’s 
also possible that the wall outlet 
may be defecƟve. Make sure all 
power cords are plugged in securely 
and that you can see the green light 
on the DS300 power block. The 
DS300 should always be plugged in 
to an available wall outlet. There 
may be as much as three hours of 
baƩery backup available, but baƩery 
backup should never be relied on as 
a primary power source.  
 
Memory sƟck 
 
 
69 
Responding to a Jam 
A ballot jam can generate several diﬀerent on-screen messages, depending on where the ballot jammed. In 
some instances, a jammed ballot has been counted but failed to fall into the ballot box. In other instances, a 
jammed ballot is not counted and is either returned to the voter or is stuck in the ballot transport. Before acƟng, 
carefully read the message on the screen to determine if the ballot has been counted. Direct the voter to view 
the message on the screen. See Ballot Counted (p. 69) and Ballot Not Counted (p. 70). 
Most ballot jams are caused by an obstrucƟon between where the ballot exits the back of the DS300 and enters 
the ballot box. 
 If using the DS300 ballot box, look into the window on the back of the ballot chute to conﬁrm nothing is 
obstrucƟng the ballot path. 
 The power cord on the DS300 may be interfering with the paper path. Unlock the front ﬂap securing the 
DS300 on the ballot box. Slide the DS300 forward and make sure the power cord does not block the 
paper path when the DS300 is being slid back into place.  
 If the ballot box is full, see Ballot Bin is Full (p. 72). 
 Keep an eye on the Public Count at the top of the screen as a ballot is fed into the tabulator to verify that 
the Public Count has incremented +1.  
 
Ballot Counted 
If the ballot is hung up at the back of the DS300 and the message on screen indicates the ballot has been 
counted, unlock the front ﬂap securing the DS300 on the ballot box. Slide the DS300 forward and if the ballot is 
visible, push it into the ballot box. If the ballot does not fall all the way into the ballot box, open the main ballot 
compartment door to see if the ballot box is full. If the ballot box is full, see Ballot Bin is Full (p. 72). 
 
  

 
70 
Ballot Not Counted 
If the message on the screen indicates a ballot is jammed but not counted, the DS300 will aƩempt to return the 
ballot to the voter. If the voter reinserts the ballot and the problem persists, inspect the ballot for wrinkles, tears 
or other damage. If the ballot is damaged, see Spoiling a Ballot (p. 33) for instrucƟons to spoil the ballot and 
issue new ballot.  
 
Ballot Not Counted Unable to Return 
If the message on the screen indicates a ballot is jammed but not counted, and the DS300 is unable to return the 
ballot to the voter, unlock the front ﬂap securing the DS300 on the ballot box. Slide the DS300 forward and 
gently pull the ballot from the back of the DS300. Be careful not to allow the ballot to drop into the ballot box as 
it would be diﬃcult to idenƟfy the ballot. If the ballot does drop in, call the Manager Hotline to report it in front 
of the voter and advise the voter that the ballots will be reconciled through the tabulator. If the ballot does not 
drop in, inspect the ballot for damage and return to voter for rescanning. If the problem persists, see Spoiling a 
Ballot (p. 33) for instrucƟons to spoil the ballot and issue new ballot. 
Emergency Compartment 
If the tabulator malfuncƟons, the emergency compartment can be unlocked and used. Using the ﬂat silver key, 
unlock the emergency compartment and fold down the metal ﬂap to allow the compartment to receive ballots. 
Call the Manager Hotline immediately to report the situaƟon. Follow the procedures for using the emergency 
compartment. See Precinct Manual for Poll Oﬃcials (Ch. 9). When the tabulator resumes proper funcƟons, open 
the emergency compartment with the ﬂat silver key and fold the metal ﬂap up to close the compartment. 
 
 Emergency compartment with ﬂap up (closed) Emergency compartment with ﬂap down (open) 
 
 
71 
Printing Errors 
The DS300 printer uses thermal paper and heat transfer to print informaƟon.  
Pink Line on Paper Tape 
A pink-Ɵnted line will appear on the paper tape when the 
roll is about to run out of tape. The tape roll should be 
replaced before it runs out. 
Use the ﬂat black key to open the access compartment. To 
replace the tape roll, push in the blue tab to release the 
tape compartment. LiŌ out the roll and put a new roll in 
with the tape feeding under the roll and to the right (if 
facing the tabulator). Snap the lid back in place. If the tape 
doesn’t print properly, you may have put the tape in 
backward. To test, pull the tape up and over to the leŌ. If 
you can scratch the tape and see a mark, you have done it 
properly. Close and lock the access compartment.  
Printer Functioning Without Visible Print 
If the printer isn’t prinƟng on the tape, it is because the paper roll is inserted incorrectly. Use the ﬂat black key to 
open the access compartment. Press the blue latch to open the tape compartment. Reinsert the tape roll. Make 
sure that a half-inch of tape is sƟcking out of the printer compartment.  
If the tape runs out while prinƟng other reports, aŌer you change the tape roll, the DS300 will begin prinƟng the 
new report with a reprint of the last 18 lines printed before the tape ran out. To match up the two secƟons of 
the report, ﬁnd the last entry on the old roll and use the Ɵmestamps to ﬁnd the repeated entries on the new roll. 
 
Error Transmitting Results 
If the results are not successfully sent, a message will be displayed. You can aƩempt to transmit results again. If 
that aƩempt is not successful, call the Technology Hotline. 
Properly loaded tape roll 
 
72 
Ballot Bin is Full 
If the ballot bin becomes full on ElecƟon Day, the ballots will need to be transferred using the following procedure: 
1. Direct voters to cast their ballots into another tabulator, if available. 
2. The Manger will say, “We are temporarily pausing the casƟng of ballots to empty the full ballot bin and 
secure voted ballots.” 
3. The Manager and another poll worker will unlock the ballot bin using the ﬂat silver key. They will remove 
the voted ballots and place them in a Red Bag. Close and lock the ballot bin. 
4. The Manager will say, “We are resuming the casƟng of ballots.” 
5. The Manager will retrieve a seal from the Extra Seals Envelope and seal the Red Bag.  
6. The Manager will retrieve the Chain of Custody Log from the AdministraƟve Folder and note the seal 
number in SecƟon 3 under “Red Bag 1 Seal #.” Return the Chain of Custody Log to the Brown Form 
Folder unƟl needed for closing procedures. 
7. The Manager will store the sealed Red Bag at the Help Desk. 
8. Make a note on the Trouble Log and noƟfy the Manager Hotline. 
 
Tabulator Replaced 
If a replacement tabulator is used, be sure to update SecƟon 2 of the Chain of Custody Log with the serial 
numbers and tracking number. The Manager and Assistant Manager should sign where indicated on the form.

Ballot Information Not Sent To ExpressVote 
While checking in a voter at the Help Desk who wishes to 
mark their ballot with the ExpressVote, you may see a 
message that ballot informaƟon could not be sent to 
ExpressVote.

To manually acƟvate an ExpressVote ballot card: 
1. At the welcome screen, insert the blank 
acƟvaƟon card into the front slot.  
2. If the elecƟon contains mulƟple precincts, the

elecƟon contains more precincts than can 
be displayed on a single screen, select 
“Next” or “Previous” to navigate the enƟre list. 
To reduce the number of displayed precincts, 
select the Search bar. Enter the precinct name, or 
the ﬁrst few characters of the precinct number or 
name, then select “Search.” The system displays 
only precincts matching the entered search 
characters. Select the correct precinct. 
3. On the ballot style selecƟon screen, select the 
correct ballot style (if any). If the elecƟon

ﬁrst page of the selected ballot so the voter can

AŌer you have assisted the voter, call the Technology 
Hotline to report the print failure and receive guidance.

C. ExpressVote 
Timed-Out Session 
AŌer ﬁve minutes of inacƟvity, such as a voter walking 
away from the ExpressVote or failing to interact with the 
screen, the screen will display the message “ConƟnue 
VoƟng?” If the voter selects “Yes, keep voƟng,” they will 
be returned to the previous screen. If they select “No, 
quit voƟng,” their ballot card will be ejected.  
 
If the voter fails to interact with the above screen for 30 
seconds, an audible alert will sound and the following 
screen will appear. To cancel a vote session on a terminal 
that has Ɵmed out, you will need to enter the 
ExpressVote code found on the Blue Polling Place Sheet. 
Then, complete the following steps.  
1. On the Ɵmeout alert screen, touch “End VoƟng.” 
The system displays the Enter ElecƟon Code 
screen. 
2. On the Enter ElecƟon Code screen, touch the on-
screen keypad to enter the ExpressVote code 
found on the Blue Polling Place Sheet, then touch 
“Accept.” The system displays the Vote Session 
Ended screen. 
3. Touch “ConƟnue.” The system displays the Vote 
Session Canceled reason screen. 
4. On the reason screen, touch to select the check 
box corresponding to the reason for this 
cancellaƟon (Wrong Ballot, Voter Request, Voter 
Abandoned Ballot, Problem with VoƟng Machine, 
Other). 
5. Touch “ConƟnue.” The system ejects the card and 
displays a Vote Session Canceled screen 
instrucƟng the poll worker to remove the ejected card. 
6. Remove the ejected card. The system redisplays the Welcome screen and is ready for the next vote 
session.  
7. If the voter is unknown, place the unscanned ballot card in the Gray Bag. If the voter is known, instruct 
the voter to insert the ballot and begin the process of marking the ballot again.

Chapter 8 – Closing Procedures 
(SecƟons 104.29, 100.011, 101.049, 102.031, Fla. Stat.) 
You may assign closing duƟes to speciﬁc poll workers so that the closing procedures may be carried out quickly 
and accurately. Use your best judgement. The ElecƟon Team must complete steps including, but not limited to, 
securing equipment and ballots.  
The public and the media are allowed to enter the polling room and watch the procedures aŌer the polls close 
and aŌer all voters have cast their ballots, but photography is not allowed. The Manager should designate an 
area for public observaƟon as the public is not permiƩed to wander around the polling room. During ballot 
reconciliaƟon, no more than 3 people are allowed to come near the elecƟon oﬃcials at the same Ɵme.  
A. Announce the Closing of the Polls 
At the scheduled closing hour at 7:00 p.m. sharp on ElecƟon Day, the Manager shall make a public 
proclamaƟon: “The polls are oﬃcially closed! Anyone in line at the Ɵme of this announcement will 
be allowed to vote.” The Ɵme indicated on the EViD is the oﬃcial Ɵme for the purpose of closing the 
polls. 
 
B. Voters in Line at Close of Polls 
All eligible voters standing in line at the polling place by 7:00 p.m. on ElecƟon Day shall be allowed to vote. The 
Deputy shall stand behind the last person in line to clearly mark the end of the line and establish a cut-oﬀ point.   
 
C. Closing Polls and Transmitting Results 
Using the Visual Aid Packing Chart as a reference, retrieve the ﬂat black key, the ﬂat silver key, round barrel key, 
Chain of Custody Log, DS300 Tape Envelope, Gray Bag, Red Bag(s), Unoﬃcial ElecƟon Results Sheet, and the 
Black Pouch and provide them to two Inspector Techs to close polls and pack up the tabulator. See Precinct 
Manual for Poll Oﬃcials, Closing Procedures: Tabulator (Ch. 7). Observe the closing procedures. 
Sealing the Tabulator 
AŌer Closing Procedures are complete and the tabulator(s) have 
been repacked, retrieve three white seals from the Extra Seals 
Envelope in the Purple Bag for each tabulator at your polling place. 
On the front of the DS300, use one to seal the tabulator, one to seal 
the emergency compartment, and one to seal the ballot bin. 
VoƟng equipment must not be shut down prior to the 
announcement of polls closing and the compleƟon of 
voƟng. No equipment may be shut down early for any 
reason. If it is determined that any equipment was shut down before 
the last voter exited the polling room, all poll workers may be 
terminated. If you see something, say something. NoƟfy the Call 
Center immediately at (904) 255-VOTE [8683]. 
  

 
 
81 
D.  Ballot Reconciliation 
(SecƟon 104.29 Fla. Stat.) 
Ballot accounƟng must be conducted on ElecƟon Day aŌer the polls close. Do not let any unauthorized person 
touch or otherwise interfere with ballots, ballot containers or the process for counƟng ballots. Do not rush 
through any process or be pressured for results. Accuracy is extremely important.  
 
Ballot Report Form 
 
82 
Completing the Final Ballot Report Form  
Retrieve the PracƟce Ballot Report Form and the Final Ballot Report Form from the Teal Bag. 
  
Work with a poll worker to complete the boƩom porƟon of the PracƟce Ballot Report Form using the 
instrucƟons below: 
1. Count unused printed ballot pads. MulƟply count by 25, enter amount on line (A). 
2. Count printed ballots aƩached to parƟally used pads and enter total on line (B). 
3. Count printed ballots in Spoiled Ballot Envelope and enter total on line (C). 
4. Count unscanned printed ballots in the Gray Bag, enter total on line (D). 
5. Count printed provisional ballots in the Orange Bag, enter total on line (E). 
6. Count printed provisional ballots in the Green Bag (if applicable), enter total on line (F). 
7. Add lines (A) through (F) and enter total on line (2). 
8. Count the number of ExpressVote ballot cards in Spoiled Ballot Envelope, enter on line (G). 
9. Count the number of unscanned ExpressVote ballot cards in Gray Bag, enter on line (H). 
10. Count the number of ExpressVote provisional ballot cards in the Orange Bag, enter on line (I). 
11. Count the number of ExpressVote provisional ballot cards in Green Bag (if applicable), enter total on line (J). 
12. Enter amount from line (1) on line (3). 
13. Enter amount from line (2) on line (4). 
14. Subtract line (4) from line (3) and enter amount on line (5). 
15. Enter total counted ExpressVote ballot cards (shown on DS300 Tape) on line (6). 
16. Add lines (5) and (6) and enter amount on line (7). 
17. Add lines (C) - (F) and line (5) and enter amount on line (8). 
 
If your ballots do not reconcile, check the box at the boƩom of the form and provide an explanaƟon (e.g., “A 
voter leŌ with their ballot, and it was noted on the Trouble Log.”). Then call the Manager Hotline to report it. 
Duval County Supervisor of ElecƟons staﬀ may call you aŌer the elecƟon for clariﬁcaƟon, if needed. 
Once you are saƟsﬁed with your PracƟce Ballot Report Form, copy it onto the Final Ballot Report Form. The 
Manager or Assistant Manager and the poll worker assisƟng with ballot reconciliaƟon must sign the Final Ballot 
Report Form. Return BOTH forms to the Teal Bag.  
Failure to complete the Final Ballot Report Form before leaving for the day may result in the 
terminaƟon of the Manager and Assistant Manager. 
 
E. Chain of Custody Log 
Retrieve the Chain of Custody Log from the Brown Form Folder. Complete the Chain of Custody Log by ﬁlling out 
SecƟons 2, 3 and 4.  
SecƟon 2 – If there were any problems or changes to your tabulator during the elecƟon, you must record them 
here. Be sure to record the new serial number and tracking numbers if you receive a replacement tabulator. You 
will also need to transcribe this new informaƟon in SecƟon 3. If you accidentally broke the orange seal, aŌer 
noƟfying the Technology Hotline, you will need to record the replacement here. 

 
 
83 
SecƟon 3 – Record the serial and tracking numbers for the tabulator(s) at your polling place. Record the three 
white seal numbers used to seal the tabulator (which will be diﬀerent from opening). Record the seal number for 
the Black Bag. Record the seal numbers for the Red Bags(s) used for marked ballots. Record the seal numbers for 
the Blue Bag with Red Handles, the Blue Bag, and the Purple Bag. Sign the log with the Assistant Manager. 
SecƟon 4 – Record the date and Ɵme of departure from the polling place to the Drop Zone. Sign the log with the 
poll worker who accompanies you to the Drop Zone, if any.  
There should be two signatures in SecƟon 1, two signatures in SecƟon 3 and one signature in SecƟon 4 (unless 
the Assistant Manager or another poll worker accompanies you to the Drop Zone). Signatures in SecƟon 2 would 
only apply if you had a subsƟtute tabulator provided.  
Place the completed Chain of Custody Log in the Completed Documents Envelope in the Yellow Bag. 
 
  
 
 Chain of Custody Log 
 
84 
 
F. Technology 
Ensure Inspector Techs turn oﬀ and pack up the MiFi hotspot, electronic poll book(s), ballot-marking device(s). 
See Precinct Manual for Poll Oﬃcials, VoƟng Systems OperaƟon (Ch. 7). 
MiFi Hotspot 
Provide the Inspector Techs with the Black Pouch.  
 
Ballot-Marking Device 
Provide the Inspector Techs with the round barrel key.  
 
Electronic Poll Book 
Provide Inspector Techs with EViD acƟvator. You will also need to pack up the EViD Edge and the ExpressVote 
printer at the Help Desk. See Packing Up the EViD Edge (p. 66). 
 
G. Packing the Bags 
Pack all bags by referring to the Visual Aid Packing Chart in the AdministraƟve Folder. See Visual Aid Packing 
Chart (p. 17).  
Pay close aƩenƟon to the chart, as some items will be packed into diﬀerent bags than they arrived in. The 
memory sƟck goes out in the DS300 but returns in Black Pouch. Also, the AdministraƟve Folder goes out in 
Yellow Bag but returns in Purple Bag. 
Any wriƩen document that is not returned in the Yellow Bag should be placed in the Completed Documents 
Envelope and returned in the Yellow Bag.  
H. Clean Up 
The Manager is responsible for the condiƟon of the polling place. All poll workers are responsible for cleaning up 
inside the polling room.  
 Collect ID badges from all poll workers and return them to the Lanyard Packet in the Yellow Bag. 
 If anything belonging to the ElecƟons Oﬃce is found to be damaged, use the blue painters tape to mark 
it and write on the tape the nature of the damage (i.e. “short in cable”). 
 Break down all voƟng booths and tables. Stack voƟng booths on pallet top side up. 
 All precinct equipment and supplies must be put away prior to leaving. 
 Place all signs into the A-frame box. 
 Place the Sign Kiosk into the Sign Kiosk box. 
 All cones, the A-frame sign box, the Sign Kiosk box, the call bell, and all direcƟonal signs should be 
brought back into the polling room and placed next to voƟng booths pallet. 
 All equipment delivered by moving company should be posiƟoned back into the same locaƟon and 
arranged in the same manner as it was found in the morning. 
 The polling place should look as clean as it did upon your arrival. 
As a Manager, you are responsible for the condiƟon of your polling place! If the polling place is not 
leŌ in the way that it was found, a cleaning service will be hired. The cost of the cleaning will be 
deducted from your pay. 

 
 
85

Thank your ElecƟon Team and dismiss them. Say, “On behalf of the Supervisor of ElecƟons I want to thank you 
for your service today. You are now dismissed!” Poll workers should not leave the polling place unƟl dismissed 
by the Manager. Leaving prior to dismissal will result in employee terminaƟon. 
 
 
K. At the Drop Zone  
Your ﬁnal responsibility is to deliver all bags to the designated 
Drop Zone immediately aŌer dismissal.

Chapter 9 – Contingency 
(SecƟons 102.014(5)(k), 102.031, 102.101, 101.733 Fla. Stat.) 
A. Power Outage in the Polling Room 
If there is a power outage at the polling place, noƟfy the Manager Hotline immediately. Determine if an 
evacuaƟon is needed.  
 
If an evacuaƟon is not needed, you may conƟnue to process voters for up to two hours using the baƩery backup 
on the EViD Edge, the ExpressVote, and the DS300.  
 
If the polling place must be evacuated, say, “The polling room must be temporarily evacuated due to the power 
outage. When the power comes back on, we will resume voƟng. You may wait outside.” If it is safe to remain in 
the polling room during the power outage, use the Paper Register to check in voters once the baƩery backup has 
been depleted. 
B. Using the Paper Register 
If there is a power outage, you will not be able to use the EViD to check in voters. Call the Manager Hotline to 
request permission to use the Paper Register. Open the White Bag and instruct the Inspector Techs to set up a 
combined Check-In StaƟon/Ballot StaƟon. For instrucƟons, see Precinct Manual for Poll Oﬃcials, Paper Register 
Procedures (Ch. 9).  
Remain at the Help Desk to assist voters as needed. AŌer the polls close, it is criƟcal that the Paper Register is 
returned to the White Bag and then placed in the Yellow Bag. 
Voter Not Found 
If a voter cannot be found on the Paper Register you must call the Manager Hotline and provide them with the 
voter’s name, date of birth and current address to determine if the voter is a registered voter, if they are at the 
correct precinct, or of any other special situaƟons apply to them.  
Name/Address Change 
Instruct the voter to complete SecƟon 1 of the MulƟpurpose Form (Voter RegistraƟon ApplicaƟon) to update their 
informaƟon. Once complete, call the Manager Hotline to make the updates to the voter’s registraƟon. If the voter 
is at the correct precinct, issue the ballot. 
Wrong Precinct 
If the voter is at the wrong precinct, retrieve a notepad from the Blue Pouch. Write down the informaƟon 
provided to you including the correct precinct number and address for the polling place. Give this informaƟon to 
the voter and say: “You are not in your assigned polling place. This Ɵcket shows the name and address of your 
correct polling place.” If the voter has any further quesƟons, advise them to call (904)255-VOTE or visit 
duvalelecƟons.gov to learn more. 
No ID Provided 
If a voter does not present a valid photo and signature ID, then the voter must vote a provisional ballot. The 
provisional ballots will not be entered on the EViD, but all other procedures remain the same. See Provisional 
Ballots (p. 46). 
 
 
87 
Absentee Requested 
If “Absentee Requested” is indicated on the register and the voter is returning the vote-by-mail ballot, the 
Inspector Tech should follow surrendered ballot procedures and then place the surrendered vote-by-mail ballot 
in the Surrendered VBM Ballot Envelope. See Precinct Manual for Poll Oﬃcials (Ch. 5). 
If the voter does not have the vote-by-mail ballot with them to return, then the voter must vote a provisional 
ballot. The provisional ballots will not be entered on the EViD, but all other procedures remain the same. See 
Provisional Ballots (p. 43). 
Voter Has Already Voted 
If “Voted Absentee,” “Voted Early,” “Voted Provisional,” “Pending: Must Vote Provisional,” “Challenged: Must 
Vote Provisional,” or “PotenƟally Ineligible” is printed in the signature column, then the voter may only vote a 
provisional ballot. The provisional ballots will not be entered on the EViD, but all other procedures remain the 
same. See Provisional Ballots (p. 43). 
Voter Signature Doesn’t Match 
If the voter’s signature does not match the ID provided by the voter, instruct the voter to complete SecƟon 4 of 
the MulƟpurpose Form (Signature Aﬃdavit) and issue the ballot. If the voter refuses to sign the Signature Aﬃdavit, 
then the voter may only vote a provisional ballot. The provisional ballots will not be entered on the EViD, but all 
other procedures remain the same. See Provisional Ballots (p. 43). 
Voter Requests Assistance 
The ExpressVote has as much as two hours of baƩery backup available in case of a power outage. If there is no 
baƩery remaining, see alternaƟve procedures in Voter Needs Assistance (p. 35). 
No Battery Left in DS300 
If there is no baƩery power remaining in the DS300, you may unlock and allow voters to cast their ballots into 
the Emergency Compartment. See Emergency Compartment (p. 70). Contact the Manager Hotline immediately 
to get a replacement tabulator. 
A provisional ballot may be oﬀered if a voter does not want to wait for call center veriﬁcaƟon in special 
voƟng situaƟons (address change, name change, etc.).  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 

 
88 
C. Extended Polling Hours 
(SecƟons 101.049, 101.048, 101.733 Fla. Stat.) 
In the rare event of a court or other order extending the statutory polling hours, every voter that votes aŌer 
regular hours must vote a provisional ballot per Florida Statute. For procedures see, Precinct Manual for Poll 
Oﬃcials, Extended Polling Hours (Ch. 9). Don’t begin Extended Polling Hours procedures unless explicitly directed 
to by the Duval County Supervisor of ElecƟons. The Green Bag contains all the necessary supplies to begin 
Extended Polling Hours. 
 
The Deputy shall stand behind the last person who is in the line waiƟng to vote at the closing hour. Any person 
voƟng aŌer the last person who was in line during the scheduled closing hour of 7:00 p.m. on ElecƟon Day must 
vote a provisional ballot. These provisional ballots must be kept separate from all other provisional ballots cast 
during the regular voƟng hours.    
 
Distributing Supplies for Extended Polling Hours 
Move most of the Provisional Ballot Envelopes from the Help Desk to the Check-In StaƟon. Move most of the 
pink secrecy sleeves from the Help Desk to the Ballot StaƟon. Remove the white secrecy sleeves from the Ballot 
StaƟon and return them to the Blue Bag. Provide clipboards and pens to the Inspector Techs. Retrieve a Green 
Bag and posiƟon it near the tabulator and assign an Inspector Tech to stand beside the bag at all Ɵmes. Voters 
will cast their provisional ballot into this bag. This bag should never be unaƩended! 
 
Converting all EViDs to the Extended Hours Setting 
You will need to change the seƫngs on all EViDs to Extended Hours. You do not need to close or restart the 
EViDs before changing this seƫng. On the EViD Home screen, select “More.” Then select “Tech UƟliƟes” and 
enter the password provided by the SOE oﬃce. Select “Extended Hours.” When asked if you are sure you want to 
turn on Extended Hours, select “Yes.”  Select “Back” to return to the Home screen. 
 

 
 
89 
You should now see “Extended Hours” at the boƩom of the screen.  
 
Repeat this process on all EViDs at your polling place. You can now begin checking in voters during Extended 
Polling Hours. 
Ballot Reconciliation 
Follow procedures for Ballot ReconciliaƟon (p. 81). Make sure to hand count the Extended Hours provisional 
ballots in the Green Bag and put on Line (F) on the Final Ballot Report Form.  
Packing the Bags 
The leŌover Extended Polling Hours supplies will be returned in the Purple Bag. 
Preparing for Drop Zone Delivery 
The Green Bag containing the voted Extended Hours provisional ballots is to be treated the same as the Red 
Bag(s) in preparing for Drop Zone delivery. Retrieve a white seal from the Extra Seals Envelope. Seal the Green 
Bag containing the voted Provisional Ballot Envelopes. On the Manager’s Drop Zone Checklist, add the seal 
number for the Green Bag. 
See Preparing for Drop Zone Delivery (p. 85). 
All Extended Hours provisional ballots must be kept separate from other provisional ballots cast during 
regular voƟng hours. Do not put regular voted ballots and Extended Hours provisional ballots in the 
same bag under any circumstances.  
 
“As an elecƟon oﬃcial you perform an invaluable civic duty by 
ensuring that all voters have a posiƟve voƟng experience. Thank you 
for your professionalism, dedicaƟon, Ɵme, and service!” 
Jerry Holland, Duval County Supervisor of ElecƟons

Glossary 
 
A-Frame Sign – A large, white, plasƟc sign used to alert voters near the footpath that approaches the polling 
room entrance.  
Americans with DisabiliƟes Act (ADA) – A federal law that sets standards for ensuring that Americans with 
disabiliƟes have equal access to public services and faciliƟes.  
Assisted Voter – A voter who requests assistance in marking their ballot due to a disability or illiteracy. Assisted 
voters may have a person of their own choice assist them or have two poll workers assist them.  
Audio TacƟle Interface – An assisƟve device or handheld keypad that enables voters with visual, cogniƟve, or 
mobility impairments to mark a ballot. 
Ballot – The oﬃcial paper, with ovals ﬁlled-in by the voter, that contains all the contests to be voted on in a 
speciﬁc precinct. See also “Ballot Card.” 
Ballot Bin– The locked compartment at the boƩom of the tabulator that contains the ballots aŌer they have 
been scanned.  
Ballot Card – The oﬃcial voter-veriﬁable paper output of a ballot-marking device used by the voter, that contains 
all the contests to be voted on in a speciﬁc precinct.  
Ballot-Marking Device – The touch-screen technology that produces a paper record for vote tabulaƟon. Provides 
all voters with a simple and inclusive method of marking their ballot that conforms to ADA guidelines. 
Ballot Style – The type of ballot issued to a voter that is predetermined by the Duval County Supervisor of 
ElecƟons according to the voter’s registraƟon record (voƟng precinct and party aﬃliaƟon).  
Ballot Report Form – The form used to account for the number of ballots received, unscanned, spoiled, 
provisional, voted (cast), used, and unused at each polling place. This form is completed by the Manager and/or 
the Assistant Manager.      
Challenged Voter – A voter whose eligibility to vote has been quesƟoned. Challenged voters may vote a 
provisional ballot.  
Deputy – An poll worker, depuƟzed by the Sheriﬀ, staƟoned outside of the polling room who maintains order at 
the polls and monitors the No SolicitaƟon Zone. 
Drop Zone – The site where designated elecƟon oﬃcials will drop oﬀ red voted ballot bags and precinct supplies 
aŌer the polls close. 
DS300 – See “Tabulator.” 
Early VoƟng – As dictated by Florida elecƟon laws, a period of 8-14 days when voters can vote in-person at any 
Early VoƟng site in the county where they are registered to vote.  
 
 
91 
ElecƟon Board – The Managers and Inspector Techs appointed to conduct an elecƟon. In all quesƟons that may 
arise before members of an ElecƟon Board, the quesƟons are required to be decided by a majority vote. 
ElecƟon Team – All poll workers, including the Deputy, that work at a given precinct during an elecƟon.  
ElecƟons Center – The locaƟon where training classes are held, elecƟon equipment is stored, and precinct 
supplies are delivered and picked up prior to ElecƟon Day. 
Electronic Poll Book – Device that parƟally automates the process of checking in voters, assigning them the 
correct ballot style, and marking voters who have been issued a ballot. May be used in place of a tradiƟonal 
paper poll book or precinct register.  
Electronic Voter IdenƟﬁcaƟon (EViD) – See “Electronic Poll Book.”  
Emergency Compartment – A locked compartment at the front of the tabulator where voters insert ballots that 
cannot be scanned due to equipment malfuncƟon.  
EViD AcƟvator – The USB drive that allows an EViD to access the Florida Voter RegistraƟon database and check-
in voters. 
ExpressVote – See “Ballot-Marking Device.” 
Florida Statutes – Enacted by the Florida Legislature, all the laws of the State of Florida, including those that 
establish the electoral process in Florida. 
General ElecƟon – An elecƟon held on the ﬁrst Tuesday aŌer the ﬁrst Monday in November in the even-
numbered years for both, the purpose of ﬁlling federal, state, and county oﬃces and for the purpose of voƟng 
on consƟtuƟonal amendments not otherwise provided by law. 
Inspector Tech – A poll worker trained to check in voters using the electronic poll book, issue ballots at the Ballot 
StaƟon and operate the tabulator. 
Manager (Assistant Manager) – The person who oversees a polling place. Referred to as a Clerk in the Florida 
Statutes and administraƟve rules. 
MiFi Hotspot– A mobile Wi-Fi hotspot used to establish a wireless connecƟon between the electronic poll books 
at a precinct and the Duval County Supervisor of ElecƟons data center allowing a voter’s status to be checked in 
real Ɵme.  
MulƟpurpose Form – Form that combines the Florida Voter RegistraƟon ApplicaƟon, DeclaraƟon to Secure 
Assistance, DeclaraƟon to Provide Assistance and Signature Aﬃdavit into one convenient form. 
No SolicitaƟon Zone – As dictated by Florida elecƟon law, the area within a 150’ radius from the entrance of the 
polling place, and within the polling room, where the solicitaƟon of voters is illegal.  
Oath of Person Entering a Challenge – Form provided to voters or Poll Watchers who challenge another voter’s 
right to vote. This form must be completed by the person challenging a voter, and the challenged voter must 
vote a provisional ballot. 
 
92 
Overvote – When a voter marks the ballot for more selecƟons in a contest than the number of vacancies to be 
ﬁlled.   
Poll Watcher – Person designated by a poliƟcal party, candidate, or poliƟcal commiƩee to observe the voter 
check-in process and the conduct of the elecƟon.  All Poll Watchers must sign up by the deadline, be approved 
by the Supervisor of ElecƟons, and meet the requirements as provided by the Florida Statutes. 
Poll Worker – Trained individual who processes voters, provides instrucƟons on the proper use of voƟng 
equipment, and solves potenƟal problems that voters encounter at ElecƟon Day polling places. 
Polling Place – The locaƟon and building where voƟng occurs in each precinct. 
Polling Room – The room where voter check-in and the casƟng of ballots take place.  
Precinct – The geographical area that designates where voters go to vote and which ballot they receive.  
Precinct Register – The paper list of the names and addresses of every ciƟzen who is registered to vote in a 
speciﬁc precinct.  
Primary ElecƟon – The nominaƟon of candidates of poliƟcal parƟes as provided by SecƟon 100.061, Florida 
Statutes. Held the Tuesday ten weeks prior to the general elecƟon. 
Provisional Ballot – A ballot cast by an individual whose eligibility to vote cannot be determined.  The validity of 
the provisional ballot is determined by the Canvassing Board provided by SecƟon 101.048, Florida Statutes. 
Results Tape – The tape that is printed on the tabulator aŌer the polls are closed that summarizes the elecƟon 
results for a precinct. 
Rovers – Field service technicians directed by SOE staﬀ to polling places to assist with technical issues.  
Sample Ballot – Paper or digital examples of each precinct’s ballots that are mailed, emailed, or distributed at 
SOE oﬃces that a voter can use to make informed selecƟons prior to voƟng their actual ballot on ElecƟon Day. 
Sample ballots are now green so you can easily disƟnguish them from oﬃcial vote-by-mail ballots. 
Secrecy Sleeve – Issued by the Inspector Tech at the Ballot StaƟon to provide privacy for the ballot. 
Service Animal – A specially-trained animal performing work or tasks to assist persons with a disability. 
EmoƟonal support animals are not the same as service animals according to Florida statute.  
Sign Kiosk – A tri-fold informaƟon board located near the Help Desk that includes informaƟon, noƟces and voter 
instrucƟons in English and Spanish. 
Solicitors – Candidate and/or campaign supporters; peƟƟoners; and news media. Allowed only outside the 150-
foot radius of the No SolicitaƟon Zone. 
Spoiled Ballot – A ballot that has been marked in error, torn, or damaged during the elecƟon, or a ballot that has 
been rejected by the tabulator. Placed in a Spoiled Ballot Envelope and accounted for at the end of the night on 
the Ballot Report Form.    
 
 
93 
Tabulator – VoƟng machine used in Duval County to tally votes, transmit results, and print elecƟon reports on 
ElecƟon Day. 
Undervote – When a voter does not make any selecƟons or makes fewer selecƟons than allowed in a contest on 
the ballot.  
Universal Primary Contest – If all candidates for an oﬃce have the same party aﬃliaƟon and the winner will 
have no opposiƟon in the General ElecƟon, all qualiﬁed voters, regardless of party aﬃliaƟon, may vote in the 
Primary ElecƟon for that oﬃce.  
Unscanned Ballot – A blank or marked ballot intended to be tabulated that was not scanned by the tabulator at 
the end of the elecƟon and will be reviewed by the Canvassing Board. 
Visual Aid Packing Chart – Color-coded chart idenƟfying the locaƟon of all precinct supplies and how to pack the 
backs during closing procedures. 
Vote-by-Mail (formerly known as absentee ballot) - A ballot that is mailed or picked up in advance of an 
elecƟon. Provides the voter the opƟon to vote without having to visit an early voƟng site or polling place on 
ElecƟon Day. Requested by any registered and qualiﬁed voter. 
Voter Accessibility Kit – Contains signature guides, sanitary ear and head covers, and other accessibility 
resources.  
Voter InformaƟon Card (formerly known as Voter RegistraƟon Card) – A card mailed to each registered Florida 
voter lisƟng voter’s name, date of birth, precinct number, party aﬃliaƟon, voter registraƟon number, date of 
issue, districts represenƟng the voter, and polling place. The card is issued for informaƟonal purposes only. 
Voter RegistraƟon Form – A Voter RegistraƟon ApplicaƟon, also known as FVRA form (Florida Voter RegistraƟon 
ApplicaƟon). This is used when a voter wishes to register to vote or to make a change to their voter registraƟon. 
It is found in SecƟon 1 of the MulƟpurpose Form.  
VoƟng Booth – A piece of equipment set-up for voters to mark their ballot in private.  
VoƟng Pass – A Ɵcket given to a voter at check in that authorizes them to receive a ballot.  
Write-in Candidate – A candidate who is qualiﬁed to run for elecƟon by way of the voter wriƟng the name of the 
candidate on the ballot.   
Zero Tape – ElecƟon tape that is printed by the tabulator and veriﬁed by the Manager and the Assistant Manager 
or Inspector Tech before voƟng begins to cerƟfy that all races have a zero total.
